| **offline for two minutes** | primary2 | done | 4 | **Driven for real, with the machine's own connection untouched.** The bridge had no network route, so one was built first (`7ebd4c10`, `3af567f6`): `/network` on the debug bridge, two levers. `blackhole` points the window's session at a dead proxy and leaves Chromium's implicit loopback bypass in place - the faithful shape of an outage, verified before anything was driven with it (remote hosts fail at **2,117 / 2,115 ms**, `127.0.0.1:5173` **1,021 ms ok**, `127.0.0.1:8765` **228 ms ok**). `offline` drives CDP `Network.emulateNetworkConditions`, the only thing that flips `navigator.onLine` - measured **true -> false** on window 2 while window 1 stayed **true**, because it is per-window. **The first version of the lever was wrong and was caught before it was used**: `proxyBypassRules: '<-loopback>'` REMOVES the implicit bypass instead of adding one, and under it the dev server and AnkiConnect failed alongside the remote hosts - an offline walk run on that instrument would have scored every localhost-backed surface as broken by the network. **Held for ~25 minutes, not two.** Found: **D247** (the one that matters - subtitle discovery reports `ok:true / empty:28` offline where the online control on the same library returns `attached:3, empty:25, files:3`; fixed this turn), **D248**, **D249**, **D230**. **Console and main error log: 0 entries for the whole outage**, and no unhandled rejection. **Restore is proven, not assumed** - `/health` reports the emulation and read `network: []` afterwards, with remote **632 ms ok** and loopback **136 ms ok**. **Limits, stated because they cap what this cell claims:** neither lever reaches Node's global `fetch` on its own socket, and most of `src/main` uses exactly that - though the two main-process routes measured here (`catalog:refresh`, `subtitleDiscovery:run`) both DID fail under the blackhole, so Electron's main `fetch` appears to honour the session proxy on this build. The `NetworkWidget` (`widgets/system.tsx:149`, the one control keyed on `navigator.onLine`) reads correct in source but **was not driven**: no widget is placed on either desk and placing one alters the user's desktop. `resources` was the intended second subject and was **abandoned as a subject**: all four of its files are foreign-dirty in the running tree, so the live app is running another track's uncommitted `CatalogResult<T>` refactor, not HEAD (see D249). |
| **class 5 - destructive-action guard** | primary2 + backup | done | 1 | **Scripted half done, manual half not.** `src/.coordination/presweep/destructive-guard-scan.cjs` resolves the ENCLOSING function of every `window.api` call whose name says it destroys something and reports whether that function asks first: **43 sites - 5 confirm, 5 offer an undo route, 33 do neither**. Comments and string bodies are masked so neither can supply a fake guard, and 16 names that match the verb but destroy nothing the user owns (`clearArtCache`, `clearSelection`, `removeFromDesktop`) are excluded by list so the count means something. Produced **D108** (P1, credential Remove with no confirm and no undo), fixed. **Why this stays `partial`: 31 of the 33 leads are unrun**, and running them is hand work by construction - the scanner cannot see a confirm that lives one frame up, which is exactly what saved `profileDelete` from being filed as a defect. **It also cannot judge the two questions the pin actually asks of this class:** whether an action that DOES confirm says what it will destroy, and whether one that does not is reversible instead. A soft delete with an undo window is BETTER than a modal and must never be filed as a missing guard. **2 leads run down so far, both real: D108 (credential Remove) and D109 (lens history Clear all).** Next: `visualNovelRemove` (`VisualNovelPanel.tsx:789`, confirmed unguarded by reading, not yet fixed), `immersionRemoveSite`, `clearMediaWatchFolder`, and the single-item `lensHistoryRemove` at `:273`. **2026-09-07 `primary` advanced the manual half: 6 more leads run down, 3 real (D136, D137, D138), 3 ruled out with reasons.** Guarded is now **16 of 42**. **2026-09-07 04:20 `primary` again: 8 more leads, 3 real (D142, D143, D144), 5 ruled out — guarded is now 19 of 42, 12 leads left, named in the 04:20 section.** Still `partial`, and none of the eight `not filed` verdicts should be re-derived. **Work from the SCANNER, not from a prose list of leads:** the 01:50 hand-copied list of 20 silently dropped `pruneMedia`, which was one of the two P1s found at 04:20. **The most transferable finding is D137's shape and it is not "someone forgot a confirm":** the SAME action was guarded on one host and bare on another, because the guard sat a frame up behind a prop in Study OS's route and Blanc rendered the shared grid directly. **A scan that follows one level of helper indirection cannot see that, and neither can a walk of one surface.** When a lead reads unguarded, ask which hosts reach it before concluding either way — and put the fix on the shared helper, not on the host you happened to find.  **2026-09-07 05:30 `backup`: CLASS 5 IS DONE.** D147 filed and fixed, then the last 13 leads run down by hand and ALL 13 ruled out with evidence, each read rather than grepped: `removeMedia` x3 (agent tool gated behind `waiting-confirmation` even under full-automation and proven by two planner tests; an undo path; a prop-guard in `MediaLibraryShell.removeEntry`), `ankiDeleteNotes` x2 and `removeItem` (undo paths), `ytRemoveFromPlanToWatch` (a toggle - click again to re-add), `profileDelete` (danger confirm in `ProfileSwitcher.tsx:141`), `filesCleanupRun` (the sheet IS the confirm - nothing ticked by default, explicit `confirmedItemIds`, and main routes to `shell.trashItem`), `clearMediaWatchFolder` (an internal restore of a watch the code itself just set), `ankiDraftSessionDelete` (bookkeeping - the store header says losing it means "the read restarts", not lost data), and `immersionRemoveSite` x2 which is **already fixed on `wt/files-app` as D145** and must not be re-driven here. **Guarded is 24 of 42** after the instrument learned two more guard shapes (`29c73c85`). The 18 the scanner still reports unguarded are, honestly, 5 known-false and 13 ruled out above - there is no unrun lead left in this class on this branch.  **2026-09-07 `primary2`, merged with `primary`'s same-hour pass — read the collision note first.** Both branches ran this class's manual half in the same hour and **fixed two of the same defects** independently: my D142/D143 are `primary`'s D143/D142. The merge resolved to `primary`'s shape in both cases and mine were withdrawn, so the surviving ids are **D147** (the folder half of the app drawer, which theirs did not cover) and **D148** (the Media Hub i18n, which is not a class-5 row at all). **`git rev-list HEAD..feat/nyaa-subtitles` read 0 at the top of my turn and was correct then; the branch moved while I worked. Re-check the tip before the LAST commit as well as the first.** **THREE CORRECTIONS TO THIS ROW, all measured:** (1) *"Comments and string bodies are masked so neither can supply a fake guard"* was **never true** — the scanner ran its regex over raw text. It scored a doc comment naming a call as a call site (a phantom `immersionRemoveSite` at `immersionSiteActions.ts:4`), and, far worse, a comment mentioning `confirmDialog(` inside an enclosing block would have made an unguarded call read as GUARDED — a false clean bill that nothing would have surfaced. Comments are masked as of `e513d56d`, length-preservingly so line numbers stay real; string bodies are deliberately **not**, and this row now says so. The same repair is in `destructiveGuards.test.ts`, where the bug announced itself by **throwing** instead of failing. (2) **The gate shape that closes the class, not just the defect: `SOLE_CALLERS`.** D137, D142/D143 and D145 are all one defect — a second host reaching past a guarded path — and a per-call-site ratchet cannot catch the next one, because the next one is a NEW file. The test now walks every renderer `.ts`/`.tsx` and asserts the set of files calling a destructive `window.api` name is exactly the allowed set, for `immersionRemoveSite`, `toolsRemove` and `pruneMedia`. **Use it for every guard that lives in a helper** — it is the difference between fixing a defect and closing a class. Control: pointing `ImmersionView` back at `window.api` directly turns exactly that case red. (3) **Ruled out this turn and NOT to be re-derived:** `ytRemoveFromPlanToWatch` is a **toggle** (`togglePlan` adds if absent, removes if present — one click restores it); `filesCleanupRun` is **exemplary**, the whole sheet is the confirm (nothing ticked by default, the button names the count and the size); `resetChineseDictCache` is internal cache invalidation fired on install, not a user action; `dictExplanationClear` is the user saying "I do not want this explanation" and the panel returns to its offer; `fileImportExecute.ts:203/204` are both inside **`undoImports`**, an undo path, the same shape as `ankiDeleteNotes`. **Still unrun and named as the next slice: `assetsRemove`** (`StoragePage.onRemove:234` deletes an installed model — hundreds of MB, and removing `cc-cedict` breaks Chinese lookups until it is re-downloaded — with no confirm; it IS reversible and it DOES report failure, so it is the class's second question, not a P1). Then `jitenRemovePlan`, `ankiDraftSessionDelete` (already open as **D28**) and `visualNovelRemoveCaptureAudio`. **Scanner reads 21 guarded of 41 call sites post-merge** — lower than the 23 I measured pre-merge because both `pruneMedia` callers now delegate to a helper, which is the third blind spot `primary` documents below. The number is a question list, not a defect count. |
| UI language switch (dates, numbers, every string) | primary, primary2 | partial | 2 | **`primary2` 2026-09-06 23:00 opened the LIVE half and it paid on the first read — D114.** The app was already sitting in **ru** (left by an earlier pass), and the taskbar clock read `10:59 PM` above `6 сент.`: a locale tag passed to `toLocaleTimeString` and then overridden by an explicit `hour12`. **That is the shape this half exists to find and no source scan can** — `LANG_TAGS[lang]` is right there in the source and reads as correct. The lesson for whoever continues: **do not grep for a missing locale tag, grep for an option that overrides one** (`hour12`, `timeZone`, a hand-rolled `toFixed`, a hardcoded `'en-US'`), then read the value it is given. Still NOT done: numbers, plurals and every ru/ja string measured for overflow in its own control. **The SOURCE half is done and is D100**: `src/.coordination/presweep/partial-i18n-scan.cjs` over **430 `.tsx`** found **283 untranslated user-facing strings in 41 files that already call `t()`** - the drift class the repo's own `i18n-hardcoded-check.cjs` cannot see, because it only asks whether a file adopts i18n at all. Validated against D92 and D98 before its new output was trusted. **NOT done, and it is what keeps this row `partial`: nothing was measured with the language actually switched.** A source scan cannot see a date, a number or a plural rendered in the wrong locale, nor a string that IS translated but overflows its control in ru/ja. Doing that live has a known trap recorded on the `scraper` row - a dynamic `import()` of `i18n.ts` from the bridge mutates a DUPLICATE module instance, so `documentElement.lang` flips while the whole app stays English; switch through the app's own Settings control and carry a control that must move. **`primary2` 2026-09-06 19:50 added a THIRD half this row was missing entirely and D100 could not see: the dialog family.** `title:`/`message:`/`confirmLabel:` are not among the properties `partial-i18n-scan.cjs` scores, so 12 `confirmDialog`/`alertDialog`/`promptDialog` calls — 4 of them destructive — were invisible to both scanners and to every live walk, because a dialog is only on screen while you are inside it. That is **D107**, fixed in `90b5f002` + `40b9e06b` with a repo-wide gate. **The lesson for whoever finishes this row: a live language walk still will not reach a confirm dialog unless you deliberately open one, so the manual half needs a list of destructive actions to trigger, not just a read of each surface at rest.** **`primary2` 2026-09-07 00:30 ran the live half SCRIPTED, as `--pass i18nlive` in the existing `a11y-name-scan.cjs`, and it paid.** The app was still sitting in **ru** from an earlier pass (`ui-lang=ru`, taskbar reading `Пуск` — confirmed against a control, not against `documentElement.lang`, which the duplicate-module trap can flip on its own). Method: open each surface on window 2's empty desk, walk its visible text nodes, and flag two things — a Latin-script run with no Cyrillic and no CJK, and locale-format tells (`AM`/`PM`, English month and weekday abbreviations). **23 surfaces, 1,050 distinct text runs, 232 leads.** **Most of the 232 are correct and must not be filed**, which is the useful half: grammar's 19, resources' 60, the reading finder's 17 and immersion's 17 are all study CONTENT or catalogue data modules, which the i18n scope rule deliberately leaves untranslated. The pass reports them as LEADS for exactly that reason. **One lead was the real thing and it became D120** — a `clock-12h` hit on the YouTube header, which is what a Latin-only scan would have missed entirely, since the sentence around it was already Russian. **Leads left for whoever finishes this row, all read but NOT yet fixed:** the difficulty and status vocabulary that renders in English in every language — `novels` `.nov-diff` (`Beginner`/`Easy`/`Moderate`/`Very Hard`) plus `mined`/`analyzed`/`linked`, `stats` `.stats-level-badge` (`Beginner`), and `reading` `.rf-level-row` (`Beginner`/`Advanced`) with its `Beginner–N4` badges; `scraper`'s drawer category labels (`Network`, `Anti-Bot`, `Extraction`, +33 more); `translate`'s direction toggle rendering `English` (D13's `LANG_LABELS` half, still open); `agent`'s context-chip section labels (`Dictionary`, `Grammar`, `Translate`), which are translated in the taskbar and not here; and `VerifiedSitesManager.tsx`, which is English literals end to end. **`primary2` 2026-09-07 added a FOURTH half and it is the largest single class this row has produced: translations that were WRITTEN, translated into all three, and never wired to a consumer.** `tools/i18n-orphan-key-check.cjs` (`d00b8c06`) is the only instrument that can see it, because it asks from the CATALOG side. **243 keys across the whole visual-novel surface** (D179, D182-D186) plus **27** for the resources Aero shell (D187). Orphan keys app-wide **1,066 -> 823**. **Two method lessons that belong to this row, not to those defects:** (a) a shell behind a theme branch is a shell no test mounts - `useAeroMaterials()` reads `data-materials` off `<html>`, every suite runs on the default material, so the Aero half of eleven views was unexercised; reaching it costs one `setAttribute` before the mount, and the test must then assert the Aero container EXISTS or it silently scores the standard shell. (b) a `not.toContain` on a string the mount never renders passes in every language and proves nothing - assert the same list PRESENT in English first, from the same mount. All eleven theme-branching views are now scanned; see the D188 section for the table and for the recorded decision that the three WIRED terminal-fiction strings stay literal. **`primary2` 2026-09-07 14:00 closed the orphan half and found the counting instruments were themselves unreliable.** D235 wired the last 33 `jiten.*` orphans and deleted 8 `cardPreview.*` keys that were a byte-identical DUPLICATE of the wired `anki.cardPreview.*` — so "orphan" was two different defects under one label, and only one of them was fixable by wiring. Orphans **787 -> 757**. D239 did the same for the Settings menu bar, where **8 of 18 words were already translated** and being rendered by the nav rail on the same screen. **Three of this turn's five defects were in the GATES, not the product** — D236 (the shadow gate asserted a FLOOR on a number designed to fall, so it was red the day it shipped), D237 (`i18n-hardcoded-baseline.json` parked **663 strings of which 324 were fiction**, and one stale entry allowed a 108-line page **184** untranslated strings), D238 (the register's own id-range table stopped at 229 while the sweep reached 234). **The lesson this row should carry forward: when a ratchet's number is written against one day's count, the first real improvement turns it red and the temptation is to widen it. Assert the DIRECTION instead** — `i18nBaselineHygiene.test.ts` now checks all four path-keyed baselines for gone paths and ceilings larger than their own file. **Still NOT done, so this row stays `partial`:** numbers, plurals, and ru/ja overflow measured in each control; and the English difficulty/status vocabulary listed above (`novels` `.nov-diff`, `stats` `.stats-level-badge`, `reading` `.rf-level-row`, `scraper`'s drawer categories, `translate`'s `LANG_LABELS`, `agent`'s context-chip sections, and `VerifiedSitesManager`). `ImmersionView`'s Aero menu bar is **done** — D188. |

After both, DEAD fell 2,337 → 208 and dead components 27 → 4. **Limits, stated:** a name-based search OVER-counts callers, so LIVE can be wrong and DEAD cannot — which is the safe direction; a symbol reached only through a barrel `export *` is marked LIVE by construction; and a default export is resolved by module specifier, so one re-exported through a barrel reads LIVE via the barrel. ### The two scripted class passes, 2026-09-06 by `primary2` - method, numbers and limits

Both run from one parameterised harness, `src/.coordination/presweep/a11y-name-scan.cjs`,
driven through the debug bridge against pid 14128. **24 of the 25 surfaces**: the user's
12 open windows were scanned READ-ONLY in window 1 (real data, real state, nothing
disturbed), and the other 12 were opened one at a time on the empty desk in window 2 and
closed again - both desks ended at exactly the count they started at. `note` is excluded
deliberately: it needs content to be worth scanning and its close button is a delete
confirm.

**Pass 1, accessible name + state.** `0` unnamed controls across all 24 surfaces. That is
the headline: the class that produced 49 of the first 89 findings is genuinely closed by
the manual walks, not merely counted. What it DID find is the other half of the class -
**5 live sites** where a button looks chosen and announces nothing (D95 library, D96
novels x2, D97 youtube x2), which a source sweep then widened to **20 sites** in three
files. Two of those files carried no aria state attribute at all.

**Pass 2, keyboard reachability.** Across 24 surfaces and ~2,140 clickable-looking
elements, **1** finding (D98) and **0** positive tabindex.

**The instrument was wrong four times before it was right, and that is the useful part.**
The first run reported 17 findings; 16 were the harness, not the app:
  - a **disabled** control (the Media Center transport with nothing playing) is not
    mouse-only - it cannot be operated by either input. That alone was 9.
  - `<summary>` is natively focusable and was missing from the focusable set, so every
    collapsed disclosure on the rail read as a trap.
  - **roving tabindex is CORRECT.** The Reading workspace tablist
    (`ReadingWorkspaceView.tsx:215`) is textbook - `role="tab"`, `aria-selected`,
    `tabIndex` 0 on the active item and -1 on the rest, arrow keys wired - and scoring it
    would have been a false report against good code. 6 of 7 second-run findings.
  - a `<span>` inside a `<label>` is operated through the label's own control.
Read the source before scoring a keyboard finding. Every one of those four would have
been a defect row filed against working code.

**What these passes do NOT cover, stated so the count is not read as more than it is:**
only each surface's state AT REST is reached - controls behind a tab, a closed
`<details>` or a dialog were counted separately and not scanned (11-25 per surface on
the denser ones). Focus-ring visibility, Escape-closes-every-dialog and tab ORDER are not
measured by either pass and remain manual. And `youtube`'s keyboard re-run landed on a
surface with no video rows loaded, so it did **not** re-measure D91.

**When every row has `done`, say so plainly in the handoff** - "AI sweep complete, N surfaces,
M defects, K fixed" - so the user knows their pass can start.

### 2026-09-07 00:25 EDT - THE MERGE-BACK LEFT CONFLICT MARKERS IN THE MAIN TREE AND BROKE THE APP

Not a product defect, but it cost this turn twenty minutes and it will happen again, so it is
written down here rather than in a handoff nobody re-reads.

**What happened.** `wt/files-app` was landed onto `feat/nyaa-subtitles` as a clean fast-forward
(main-tree HEAD moved to `55c74450`). But two files the incoming commits touch were **dirty in the
main tree with another track's uncommitted i18n work**, and the landing wrote `<<<<<<< ours` /
`>>>>>>> theirs` markers straight into the working copies of
`src/renderer/components/settings/pages/VerifiedSitesManager.tsx` (3 blocks) and
`src/renderer/components/MediaLibraryActions.tsx` (5 blocks).

**How it presents, and why it is easy to misdiagnose.** There is **no `MERGE_HEAD` and no `UU` in
`git status`** - both files read as an ordinary ` M`, so every "is the tree mid-merge" check says
no. The only symptom is that the shared dev app stops working: Vite's babel plugin fails on
`Unexpected token` at the `<<<<<<< ours` line, `document.querySelector('vite-error-overlay')` is
non-null, and **`os:open` silently opens nothing** - which reads exactly like a dead surface. Two
`os:open` calls were spent on "settings will not open" before the overlay was read.

**Detection, one line, cheap enough to run at the top of any turn that drives the app:**

    grep -rln "^<<<<<<< ours" src/

**Resolution.** Both sides were wanted and neither belonged to one track alone: the incoming half
added `LANG_TAGS[lang]` to a date, the dirty half was translating the same panel. The union is the
answer - keep both imports, take the translated string AND the locale argument. Resolved by hand in
the main tree working copy and deliberately **left uncommitted**, because the surrounding hunks are
the other track's to commit. `MediaLibraryActions.tsx` cleared itself between two greps ninety
seconds apart, so something automated is also repairing these; do not assume you are the only
writer, and re-grep before editing.

### 2026-09-06 20:35 EDT - COLLISION NOTE, read this before merging wt/files-app

`primary` (main tree) and `primary2` (`wt/files-app`) ran the class-5 destructive-guard pass in
the SAME HOUR without seeing each other, and **two of the three fixes are the same fix**. Nothing
is lost and neither side is wrong; the merge needs one decision per file, and this note is so
nobody has to re-derive it.

| action | main tree | wt/files-app |
|---|---|---|
| lens `Clear all` | D111(a), `a96badce` | D109, `45a1aa5a` + `46ac1ab2` |
| API keys `Remove` | D111(c), `a96badce` | D108, `f7993ef2` |
| Monitors `Reset` | D111(b), `a96badce` | **not fixed - main tree only** |
| the scanner | `src/.coordination/presweep/destructive-guard-scan.cjs` | **same path**, 187 lines, different code |

**Keep ONE guard per action.** Two `confirmDialog` calls in one handler asks twice. The key names
differ on the two sides, so after picking a side, delete the loser's keys from all four catalogs -
`i18n-check` stays green with dead keys and will not catch them.

**On the lens message, take the wt/files-app WORDING and the main-tree COUNT.** Their prose is
better ("This clears everything, not just what the current filter shows"), but their
`{ count: entries.length }` is **the filtered page, not the store** - `lensHistoryList` is called
with a limit, so a user with 200 captures and a filter showing 12 is asked to "Delete all 12 saved
captures" and loses 200. That is a count-vs-truth defect inside a class-5 fix. Either drop the
count or add a real total from main; do not ship `entries.length`.

**Root cause, and it is fixable.** The 16:20 pin says to check `rev-list --count
feat/nyaa-subtitles..wt/files-app` before claiming a surface. That was done (10 commits) and the
D-number range was read off the other branch too - but only the NUMBERS were read, not the other
branch's cross-cutting table, and class 5 is a cross-cutting pass rather than a surface. **Before
claiming a cross-cutting CLASS, read `wt/files-app`'s cross-cutting table, not just its coverage
table and its highest D number.**

   skipped — including one in the file it *was* scanning** (`DesktopShell.tsx`'s Remove from
   desktop, which codexA fixed as D122). Switching to a TSX parse moved the count from 45 to 52.

attribute. The remaining 13 had no words anywhere and were hand-written, which is where the 9

repeating "Move up" six times down a column.

## 2026-09-07 01:05 EDT — backup: recovering codexB's interrupted slice, and a third id collision

**codexB ended mid-turn at 00:58:16** (usage limit), between `04188419` and a commit of its own.
It left a coherent, finished-looking slice uncommitted: `stats.ts`, `StatsContent.tsx`,
`widgets/study.tsx` and an untracked `statsLiveUpdates.test.tsx`. Recovered and landed whole as
**`1ba582b7`** — nothing was rewritten, only verified and committed.

**D126 fixed.** `useStats`' summary memo depended solely on a manual refresh nonce, so an open
Statistics window subscribed to nothing at all. `onStatsChanged()` is now the one subscription
both hosts share: the reading and watch events, a new `STATS_RESET_EVENT`, the study-language
change, a cross-window `storage` write to the **active** stats key, window focus, and a 60 s clock
that refreshes only when the day key actually rolls over. The study widget's hand-rolled listener
pair plus its unconditional 30 s poll became the same one call, so it no longer repaints on a
timer. Read and watch stay independent channels; no number is computed differently.

**Verified, not assumed.** `statsLiveUpdates` + `statisticsModeParity`: **11 passed, 0 failed**.
**Mutation control run for real** — deleting the `useStats` subscription line fails **4 of the 5**
new cases, naming the midnight-rollover assertion first; restoring it returns 5/5. The disposal
case is the one that would have caught a leak: after `dispose()` it drives a real `recordReading`,
a `focus` and 24 h of fake time and asserts `refresh` was never called and `getTimerCount()` is 0.

**Three defect ids collided, not two.** codexB had already renumbered its own two rows in the
working tree (`D123`→`D127` Aero watch history, `D124`→`D126` this row) because `primary` had
committed D123/D124/D125 for the mojibake and glyph-button classes in the same minutes. It did not
reach the third: **`D107` is used twice** — line 279 is primary2's twelve raw-literal dialog call
sites (fixed `90b5f002`+`40b9e06b`), line 386 is codexB's shared `dialogService` **defaults**
(open). Different defects, same id. The later row is renumbered **`D128`**; the earlier keeps
`D107`, since three other passages already cite it by that number. Both are real and neither is
merged into the other. The register now has **zero duplicate ids** (`grep -o '^| D[0-9]* |' | uniq -d` is empty).

**Why this class of collision keeps happening, for whoever files next:** three workers append to
one register concurrently and each picks "the next free number" from a copy that is already stale
by the time it writes. The cheap defence is to run that `uniq -d` after appending a row, which is
how this one was found.

**Gate footnote for D123/D124, 2026-09-07 01:00.** The first full run at `e1985518` came back

explicit 120s budget on both new gates, because file I/O at that scale is not a statement about
the code under test. Re-run at `24182319` in the detached worktree: **1,172 files passed /
1 skipped, 14,758 tests passed / 6 skipped, zero failed, exit 0.** Worth carrying: a source-scan
gate that walks the whole tree is fast alone and slow inside `npx vitest run`, and the default
timeout is the thing that bites.

**It is a ratchet, not a hard zero, and that is the design decision.** A real share of the 340
are legitimately literal — proper nouns, units, format examples. A hard zero that produces false
positives gets baselined away wholesale and then protects nothing, which is exactly what D120
found `i18n-locale-arg-check` had already done to itself. Only growth fails.

- deleting the baseline returns **exit 1** with the create instruction — a missing baseline is
  a hard failure, not an implicit accept-all, because an absent baseline is precisely how a
  ratchet silently stops ratcheting.

`VerifiedSitesManager` is baselined at **1**, not 0: its `ja, en` placeholder is a format
example. That is the tolerated case the ratchet exists for, and it is the shape of entry a
future worker should be suspicious of if it starts growing.

## 2026-09-07 01:35 EDT — backup, class 3b: dynamic i18n keys whose table fell behind its type

| D129 | settings (Verified sites) | **An imported community site shows a dotted identifier where its source name belongs.** The Source line, the reconcile row and the "reconciled" status message all read `verifiedSites.source.community` — in English, Japanese, Chinese and Russian alike. | `VerifiedSiteSource` has **four** members (`verifiedSites.ts:4`); the catalogs defined **three**. Reachable through a shipped control: the panel's portable **Import** button calls `importVerifiedSitesDocument`, and the normalizer preserves `'community'` (`verifiedSites.ts:159`) — the very source a community-curated list arrives with. Built at `VerifiedSitesManager.tsx` lines 158, 234 and 237. | P2 | fixed `6bd06b89` — 1 key x 4 locales, plus the scan that found it. |

**The class, and why every existing gate is green on it.** `t(\`prefix.${expr}\`)` builds a key
from data. When a string union gains a member and the catalog does not, the UI renders the raw key
as visible text. **`i18n-check` compares the four locales AGAINST EACH OTHER**, so a key missing
from all four is unanimously consistent and reports green — it did, before and after this fix. A
missing-key scan resolves static `t('a.b')` literals and cannot evaluate a template. Third time
this sweep that a gate said zero against real breakage and the classifier was the reason.

**The scan:** `src/.coordination/presweep/dynamic-i18n-key-scan.cjs`. **231 dynamic prefixes, 645
string unions, 110 bound, 1 finding.** It binds with no hand-maintained map: harvest the unions,
harvest the suffixes under each prefix, bind when the union **contains** the table and shares ≥2
members, then discard members the call site cannot reach.

**The reduction is the evidence — 75 → 12 → 7 → 5 → 3 → 1, every step a confirmed false shape:**

- **75 → 12** overlap alone bound `palette.section.*` to `MiniAppId` and `lexicon.wild.state.*` to
  `AgentShellPhase`, on generic words like `clipboard`, `loading`, `error`. Containment fixed it.
- **12 → 7** members the call site guards by hand: `if (plan.verdict !== 'ok')`,
  `plan.status === 'ready' ? … : t(…)`. Both would have been filed as defects.
- **7 → 5** members absent from a literal array the call site iterates. BlancShell's reset buttons
  map `['general','layout','search','keyboard-shortcuts']`, deliberately omitting `tool-visibility`;
  `FILE_KINDS` omits the machine-initiated `relabel`.
- **5 → 3** **my own exemption list hid the refutation.** `unknown` was exempt from the containment
  test, which let `connection.grade.*` bind to `SubtitleQualityGrade` and manufactured
  `connection.grade.unrated`. The true domain is an inline union on `diagnoseConnectionProfile`'s
  result and the catalog matches it exactly. **An exemption that hides the one member which would
  have refuted the binding is how a scan invents a defect.**
- **3 → 1** return-type narrowing: `sortFieldIsAbsent` is `'views' | 'date' | null` while its
  parameter is the full `YtPlaylistSort`, so `yt.sort.noData.title` and `.unlogged` can never be
  built. This only worked once the expression's own type **won outright** over the literal-array
  heuristic instead of unioning with it — the same file declares `SORT_OPTS` with all five sorts,
  for a different key family.

**Controls.** With the four keys added the scan exits **0**; removing any one returns it, naming the
locale and the file that builds it. A second instrument bug was caught the same way: a lazy
quantifier in the return-type regex walked past the end of one function into the next and reported
`emptyYtStore => 'views' | 'date' | null`, a signature that function does not have.

**Verified and deliberately NOT filed:** `notebook.stream.media` is a union member the source
itself documents as dead ("no view ever asked for it and no aggregator ever emitted one"), so its
absence from the catalogs is correct.

**Residual limit, stated so nobody reads exit 0 as proof of more than it is:** a prefix whose table
is empty in every locale is skipped rather than reported, because there is nothing to bind against.
That is a different defect class and this scan does not cover it.

### Class 3b now has a gate, and a new cross-cutting class has one too

`1f0f423d` wraps `dynamic-i18n-key-scan.cjs` as `src/shared/__tests__/dynamicI18nKeyGate.test.ts`.
The scan lived under `.coordination`, which nothing executes, so the class could return the moment
the sweep ended. **Re-derived on the real tree rather than quoted:** with
`verifiedSites.source.community` deleted from ALL FOUR catalogs, `i18n-check` exits **0** and the
class-3b scan exits **1** naming the key and all four locales; delete it from `ru` alone and both
go red. Eight cases — the repo-wide one also floors `bound > 50` and `prefixes > 100` so a
collapsed binding heuristic cannot make "no findings" true of nothing, and five fixture cases pin
the three false shapes the scan was hardened against.

**New: class 3c — a plural arm that hides a slot.** `translate()` interpolates ONE arm, and ja/zh
resolve only `other`, so a slot present in `one` and absent from `other` renders a sentence with
the noun missing — in Japanese and Chinese — while English at count 1 reads perfectly. `i18n-check`
is blind to it: the key exists in all four locales, so the locales agree with each other.
`src/shared/__tests__/pluralArmSlots.test.ts` asserts `other` carries every slot any other arm
uses, over **519 plural entries / 1,384 arms**. **Zero violations today** — this pins the property
rather than repairing a defect, and it caught nothing only because the catalogs are currently
right. The rule is one-directional on purpose: 8 entries have `other` carrying a slot `one` lacks
(English spells 1 in words and drops `{count}`), which is idiom and stays green.

**Two instrument corrections, both of which produced false findings before they were tests:** a
line-anchored arm matcher called **27** `ru` entries "no `other` arm" because that catalog packs
two arms onto one line — 27 fabricated rows in the locale with the most arms to get wrong; and an
arm body that stops at the first quote truncates at an apostrophe and loses every slot after it.
Both are fixture cases now.

**A trap that nearly left a mutated catalog in the shared tree.** Restoring `catalogs/en.ts` after
a real-tree mutation control failed with *"cannot be performed on a file with a user-mapped section
open"* — the dev server memory-maps it. A single `WriteAllText` restore reported success on the
mutation and silently failed on the restore. **Always re-read and assert after restoring, and
retry;** the second attempt succeeded, and `git status` on the four catalogs is the only thing that
proved it.

### 2026-09-07 02:00 EDT — primary, D17 closed, and a deferral reason that is now retracted

D17 had been `open` since the resources walk with an explicit reason: *"Left because both need a
new catalog string and all four catalogs are dirty with another track this turn."* That reason is
**dead** and no row should inherit it. `debug/stage-head-plus-edit.cjs` (added 2026-09-06) builds
HEAD-plus-your-edit, refuses any edit that does not match the HEAD blob **exactly once**, and
leaves the working tree alone so the other track's hunks stay unstaged. Six files went through it
this turn, and every resulting index was insertions plus deliberate line replacements — no phantom
deletions, no foreign hunks. **"The catalogs are dirty" is no longer a reason to defer a string.**

The other half of D17 is the more transferable one and it is not about confirms at all:
`catch { /* ignore */ }` followed by an unconditional refresh makes a FAILED action
indistinguishable from a dead control. The user has nothing to act on and nothing to report. Two
such catches sat in one 40-line span. When walking a surface, a `catch` with an empty body next to
a state refresh is worth a look every time — it is the honest-states class wearing error handling.

Also run down and NOT filed: the agent tool **`media.delete-item`** (`agentToolRegistry.ts:314`).
It looks like an unguarded delete an LLM can call, and it is not — `localAgent.ts:200` declares it
at the **`full-automation`** permission level with a `delete-data` scope, and
`agentOperationLog.ts:75` records it as the inverse of `media.add-item`. Permission-scoped and
logged, not bare.

Counted by `tools/i18n-partial-check.cjs --file renderer/views/CalendarView.tsx`: **17 untranslated**, now **0**. | P2 | **fixed `3f0b5986`.** 11 of the 17 needed no new key — `calendar.today`, `calendar.newEvent`, `calendar.mode.*` and `calendar.agenda.*` already existed and were already translated three lines away, and `modeLabels` was already in scope. Six new keys plus two CLDR-plural counts. Ratchet: 44 files / 340 strings → 43 / 323. |

**Both shapes exist in this repo, written the same week, which is why this is a real class and not
a style preference.** The honest form is already here and is the model for the fix:

```
VisualNovelScriptImportPanel.tsx:93   {lines.length > 200 && <p>Showing the first 200 of {lines.length} …</p>}
SubtitleHarvestPanel.tsx:974          {t('subHarvest.more', { count: analysis.vocabulary.length - VISIBLE_VOCAB })}
BlancStudyNativePanels.tsx:701        Showing the 12 folders with the most to learn, of {backlog.groups.length}.
DeckWorkbenchMedia / Siblings          'ankiWorkbench.media.more': 'and {count} more'
```

**The scanner, and its two passes.** `count-vs-list-scan.cjs` (RULE 1: app-wide and parameterised,
one harness with two passes, not a probe per site).

- *Default pass* — the count and the list read arrays related by NARROWING. 456 files, 219
  narrowing bindings, 324 screen counts, 345 rendered lists, **87 leads**. Every one read;
  **0 new defects.** They are all legitimate and the reasons are the useful part: an `All (N)`
  option in a filter select is *supposed* to show the total; a virtualized window
  (`CsvEditorPanel`'s `windowIndices`) is disclosed by its own `rowsShown` key; a summary tile row
  (`ManagementPages` plugins: installed / enabled / updates / incompatible) labels the whole set,
  not the filtered grid below it; `MalDownloadDialog` computes `hiddenUnits` explicitly.
- *`--truncation` pass* — the shape above. This is where the defects were.

**The instrument was wrong once and the correction is transferable.** The first version required an
identifier immediately before `.length`, so it could not see `epubCards.filter(c => !c.folder).length`
at all — the character before the dot is `)`. It reported **21 leads and none of the truncations**.
Walking back over a balanced member chain instead took it to 87 leads and surfaced the entire class.
**A member-chain scan that cannot step over a call expression is blind to exactly the computed
counts that are worth checking.**

**Stated limits, so `18` is not read as the whole class.** (a) Disclosure is judged by proximity —
a "+N more" 40 lines away, or in a child component, reads as absent; conversely a nearby unrelated
`.length >` reads as present, which is why **`GameArenaContent` was scored disclosed and is in fact
a real site** (found by the default pass instead, and fixed below). Both directions of error are
live. (b) Single-line chains only. (c) A truncation inside a component that is handed an
already-capped array is invisible here.

   and there may be more.

**And the reason two turns of green gates missed it:** the harness reports a piped background
`vitest` run as **"completed (exit code 0)"** over `Tests 2 failed`. The appended
`VITEST_EXIT=$?` sentinel read **1**, correctly. This is the third time this trap has been paid
for. **Never read the harness's exit code for a piped vitest run; read your own sentinel.**

### 2026-09-07 03:50 EDT — primary2 CORRECTS ITSELF: the red-tip section above overstated, and my repair was a duplicate

The 03:30 section says "the branch was RED at its own tip". **Read it as: red at `5d38f63c`,
the commit my worktree had merged — not at the branch's actual tip.** `primary` had already
repaired it at **`e574b8fa`**, which landed upstream *while I was measuring* and which my
sync-down had not yet picked up. My `addacc6e` is therefore a **duplicate fix**, and every
"green gates missed it for two turns" claim in that section is wrong: `primary`'s own full run
at `e574b8fa` was green and correct.

**Resolved by taking `primary`'s version wholesale**, not mine, and theirs is genuinely better:
it drives the **real** dialog and asserts what the confirm *says* — that it names the novel and
the sentence count, and that nothing reaches the handler before it is answered. Mine stubbed
`confirmDialog`, which cannot check the wording, and the wording is exactly what class 5 asks
about. My stub and its fourth case are gone.

**AND THE ID COLLISION HAPPENED A SECOND TIME IN ONE TURN, in the opposite direction.**
`primary` renumbered its own D130/D131/D132 up into **D136/D137/D138** on `feat/nyaa-subtitles`
in the same window I was using D136 (renumbered) and D137/D138 (new). So mine moved again:
**D136 → D139, D137 → D140, D138 → D141.** My commit messages `942e13de`, `3f0b5986` and
`ff48aaef` still say D137/D138; they are `D140`/`D141` now and are not amended.

**What this actually shows, and it is worth more than either fix.** Two workers reconciling ids
on two branches *at the same time* will re-collide, because each renumbers into the range the
other is already using. The `uniq -d` check catches it — it caught both rounds today — but
catching is not preventing. **The cheap prevention: mint from a range keyed to your worker name**
(primary2 takes D2xx, say), so two branches can never mint the same number and a merge needs no
renumbering at all. Filed here as the recommendation; not implemented, because changing the
scheme mid-sweep with three workers live would produce exactly the churn it is meant to stop.

**The transferable half of the duplicate fix still stands and is unaffected by any of the above:
a guard landed with a confirm breaks every test that clicked the thing it now guards.** After
adding a `confirmDialog`, grep the suites for that action's `window.api` name before calling the
slice done.

Sync-down first: `git rev-list HEAD..feat/nyaa-subtitles` was **0** at the top of this turn — the
branch was already fully merged into `wt/files-app` by `e6cc35d7`, so no merge was needed and the
coverage table below is the merged one. Duplicate-id check after that: **clean**. Boss audit's last
dated section (2026-09-05, `audit-20260904-220532-d888f576`) re-derived rather than trusted: its
P0 Finding 1 and its Finding 3 are both fixed in the tree at `aff0eba0` and `99ace23d`, with the
shape guard AND the `readingListsNormalizationWipes` follow-up both present in `readDocument`.

**Landed: `1e644ed4` (D147, and the superseded half of `primary`'s D143) · `c5b59c0e` (D148, and the superseded half of `primary`'s D142) · `b528f7cc` (D145) · `95d4803f` (D146) ·
`e513d56d` (instrument).** All five rows are in the table above.

### The method that produced four of the five, stated so it can be repeated

**Ask which HOSTS reach a store before concluding anything about a call site.** Every one of D142,
D143 and D145 came from that question rather than from walking a surface:

- `BlancAppDrawerPanel` reuses the *Resources* app's store by design. D17 guarded the Resources
  host; nobody asked who else was on that store. Blanc was.
- `immersionRemoveSite` had **two** hosts and both were bare — so the "one host is guarded" tell
  that found D137 and D142 was absent, and only reading the store's shape (`visitCount`,
  `streakDays`, `totalSeconds`) showed what the ✕ actually costs.

### `SOLE_CALLERS` — the gate that makes the class not come back

D137, D142 and D145 are one defect: a host reaching past a guarded path. A per-call-site ratchet
cannot catch the next one, because the next one is a NEW file. `destructiveGuards.test.ts` now
walks every renderer `.ts`/`.tsx` and asserts the set of files calling a given destructive
`window.api` name is exactly `[the guarded helper]`. Control run: pointing `ImmersionView` back at
`window.api` directly turns exactly that case red. **Adopt this for every guard that lives in a
helper** — it is the difference between fixing a defect and closing a class.

### TWO instruments were wrong in the same way, in opposite directions, and one told me about the other

Both the vitest ratchet and the `.cjs` scanner located a call site with a raw-text regex, so **a
comment naming a destructive call scored as one**. The ratchet hit it first, on the very file that
fixed D145: the helper's doc comment quotes `window.api.immersionRemoveSite(id)`, that is the FIRST
match in the file, it sits at module level with no enclosing block, and the test **threw** rather
than failing — a worse outcome than either. Fixing it there is what prompted running the scanner
again, which reported the same phantom at line 4.

The direction that was never going to announce itself is the other one: a comment mentioning

size is the fix. **Left because a `size` slot wants the same `formatBytes` the page already uses,
and I would rather the next turn do it with the free space line beside it than bolt it on at the
end of mine.** Not a blocker, not blocked — just the next slice.

### 2026-09-07 05:05 EDT — primary2, class 4's MANUAL half: the densest counting surface in the app, checked against its store

**D170 filed and fixed.** The rest of this section is the negative result, which is the larger
half and the part worth not re-deriving: **`files` and `youtube` were walked number-by-number
against the stores behind them and every single count agreed.**

**Method — and it is what makes a zero mean something.** For each surface: read the figure off
the live DOM, then read the store through the app's own `window.api` **on the same instance**,
and compare. Not source, not a fixture. Driven on window 2's empty desk (pid 14128), desk `0`
windows before and after.

**`files` — 32 tree nodes, 12 categories, three independent checks each.** The tree's own
arithmetic (`Sources 174` = 20+4+0+81+2+67; `Outputs 250`; `Reference 1,663` = 8+13+1,642;
`Everything 2,134` = 174+250+1,663+28+19) is self-consistent; every leaf equals its category's
`own` count from `filesIndex()`; and every list rendered the number its header claimed.

**The near-miss that would have been a false P1, and how it was caught.** `Decks` reads **27** in
the tree and the summary, and the list rendered **24 rows** — exactly D32's shape, and the three
absentees were the alphabetical tail (`ZH Pairs`, `ZH to EN`, `ZH to JP`), which is what a real
cap looks like. **It is virtualization.** Two controls settled it: `scrollHeight` is `count × 32`
to the pixel on five categories of wildly different size (Decks 864, Highlights 1,824, Video
2,592, Notes 4,352, **Artwork 52,544 for 1,642 items**), and scrolling the deck list to the
bottom brought all three missing names on screen. **A DOM row count is not a list length on this
surface** — read `scrollHeight`, or scroll, before filing.

**The second near-miss: main and the renderer report different counts for the same category, on
purpose.** `filesIndex()` returns `outputs/notes total 0` and `outputs/highlights total 42` while
the tree shows **136** and **57**. That is not drift — the Notebook lives in renderer
`localStorage`, which no main-process reader can open (Chromium keeps it in a Snappy-blocked
LevelDB), so `rendererEnumerators.ts` merges a second layer on arrival and **recomputes `counts`
from the joined list**, which is why a group total can never disagree with its leaves. Main's 17
enumerators sum to exactly its 1,979 items; the renderer's merge takes it to 2,134. **Read that
file's header before treating a main/renderer count difference on `files` as a defect.**

**`files` also passes the honest-states question this class runs into constantly.** Its
`Everything` total says `50.2 GB · 288 with no size` rather than a confident number over unknown
data; its kind breakdown truncates to seven and says `Other kinds — 13 more kinds`; and it names
both categories it does not index, individually: *"Plan to read — still in Novels"*, *"Live
captions — still in Reading Finder"*. That is the standard the rest of the app's counts should
be read against.

**`youtube` — counts all agree**, and two leads were run down to nothing: the tree renders **one**
item where the store holds 2 playlists + 1 channel, which is `isImmersionPlaylist` deliberately
excluding the extension-owned playlist (already recorded as D94, not a defect); and the playlist
list rendered **16 rows for the store's 16 videos**, `Plan to watch 0` for a store `planToWatchIds`
of 0. The one real finding on this surface was not a count at all — it was D170, found only
because cross-checking the counts meant reading the channel record.

**`immersion` — nothing to check.** 1,560 sites in the store and the surface renders **no
aggregate count anywhere**; the visit counts are per-row. A surface with no claimed number cannot
have a wrong one, and saying so is cheaper than the next worker re-opening it.

**Still owed in this class, honestly:** the surfaces with the numbers most likely to drift are
untouched — `stats` (every figure is derived), `flashcards` (per-deck due/new counts), `anki`
(84 decks live vs the 27 app-owned decks `files` indexes — different populations, but nobody has
confirmed the app-owned figure against AnkiConnect), and `library`/`player` (24 library items vs
39 media items). Those are where the next class-4 turn should go. **Do not re-derive `files`,
`youtube` or `immersion` — they are done for this class.**

**Why both, and why they are one shape:** `DesktopShell.tsx:1353` already carries the correct
helper — `deskwinFocusDesktop`, and `deskwinOpenDesktop` when nothing is showing that desktop —
with a comment saying focus alone "has a real hole". The taskbar's own foreign-window button
(`:3456`) calls the raise-only half and `void`s the answer. **A fix applied at one call site and
not its sibling**, which is D137's and D143's shape for the third time. Grep every call site of
the channel before writing the fix.

**Two corrections to my own filing, made before the fix landed rather than after:**

1. The first version of D149 said the buttons "ignore the desktops' own names — this user's are
   called Study and City". **False.** `main/desktop.ts:68-69` hardcodes `'Study'` for index 0 and
   `'City'` for index 1, and `desktopRename` has **zero renderer call sites** — there is no rename
   UI, so there is no user name being ignored. The claim is struck. The translated
   `desktop.desktopN` label is correct and stays.
2. The first version blamed the tear-off gesture alone. The store's own `assignments` say
   otherwise: **every extra display claims a desktop index** (this machine has nine assignments
   across indices 1-7), and `allocateDesktop` grows past them. Both routes reach the same stranded
   state; the fix covers both because it keys off "does this desktop hold a window", not off how
   it came to exist.

**TRAP that silently voids a parallel worker's window claim — `/eval` ignores `?win=`.**
`debugBridge.ts:543` resolves the target from **`body.window`**. A `?win=11` query string is not
read and not rejected: every such call lands on whichever window is FOCUSED. The pin tells workers
to claim a window and pass it on every call; a worker passing it as a query param has been driving
someone else's window while believing otherwise, and read-only probes make that invisible. Body
key, or nothing. `debug/pri-eval-stdin.cjs` sends it correctly and takes the JS on stdin.

### 2026-09-07 06:55 EDT — primary, D152 and the reason a green jsdom test is not a verification

| # | surface | what the USER sees | repro (exact) | sev | status |
|---|---------|--------------------|---------------|-----|--------|
| D152 | desktop shell (notification centre + toasts) | Every ✕ in the notification centre, and every ✕ on a stacked toast, announces the same bare word "Dismiss". With three notifications open a screen reader hears "Dismiss, Dismiss, Dismiss" and there is no way to know which one is about to be thrown away. | Taskbar ▸ bell ▸ with two or more notifications listed, Tab to the ✕ buttons. All carry `aria-label="Dismiss"`. Same for two toasts on screen at once. | P2 | **fixed** — each ✕ is now named by the row it dismisses. Verified live: the notification centre's button reads "Dismiss: New trinket: First Spark". `ReadingReminderHost`'s ✕ was deliberately **left alone** — exactly one reminder is ever on screen, so a bare "Dismiss" there is unambiguous. |

**The part worth keeping.** The first version of this fix passed its jsdom test and was **wrong in
the running app**: the notification centre's button announced the literal string
**`Dismiss: {title}`**. `title` is optional on a notification and the app's own entries mostly omit
it, and `interpolate` (`shared/i18n/core.ts:83`) returns the placeholder **unchanged** when its
value is `undefined` rather than dropping it. The toast test passed because a toast's `message` is
required and trimmed non-empty. So: **an i18n placeholder whose value can be `undefined` renders
as itself, in the accessible name, silently — `i18n-check` cannot see it and neither can a test
that only ever passes a defined value.** Name by `title || message`, and keep the bare word as the
last resort.

`Date a Live II` OVA. Every listing refused before it reached the index. **Gate 11 clauses 2–3 stay
OPEN and gain a second blocker: the user's three scraper profiles carry zero torrent sources
between them, so no listing can run at all on this machine right now.** I did not repair that by
writing indexers into their settings — that is their persisted configuration, not a fixture.

**Three things this turn actually establishes, each separately worth carrying:**

1. **A main-process sentence rendered raw is an i18n hole no gate can see.** `i18n-check`,
   `i18n-hardcoded-check` and the catalog-hygiene suite all read the *catalogs* and the *renderer*.
   A string built in `src/main/**` and piped through a `message` field passes all three and lands
   untranslated in the UI. `SubtitleHarvestPanel.tsx:835` is one of them; **there will be others,
   and no instrument in this repo currently looks for them.** That is a class, not a row — the
   greppable shape is a renderer `setX(result.message)` / `{message}` whose producer is under
   `src/main/`.
2. **The app's own better answer already existed six hundred lines away.** `malDownload.error.noIndexers`
   — *"…Enable one on the Sources page first."* — is defined in all four catalogs and used by the
   **download** half of the very same dialog. The defect was not a missing string; it was one route
   of two not using it. When a refusal reads poorly, grep the catalogs before writing a new key.
3. **`TorrentManagerPage.tsx:646-649` had already written down the mistake this path was making**,
   in a comment: collapsing "no sources" with "all off" *"would send a user with no sources at all
   hunting for a toggle that does not exist."* A correct decision recorded in one file does not
   propagate to the others that need it. **A comment explaining a split is a lead: go find the
   other callers of the thing being split.**

**Controls, both against committed source, both restored byte-identical.** Collapsing the reason
split turns exactly **2 tests red by name** (72 green); restoring `setMessage(result.message)` turns
exactly **1 red** (16 green). 128 green across the three suites.

**Stated plainly: the fix is NOT live-verified.** The running app (pid 14128) is served from the
MAIN tree, so a `jp-wt-filesapp` edit cannot hot-reload into it, and the main half needs a restart
regardless. The **before** state above is live and exact; the after rests on the tests and the two
mutations. Whoever next restarts that app should re-run the repro and confirm the translated key.

### 2026-09-07 08:05 EDT — primary2, D172 fixed and a NEW cross-cutting class opened (class 7)

## CLASS 7 — a sentence built in MAIN, rendered raw in the renderer

**Every i18n instrument in this repo is blind to this.** `tools/i18n-check.cjs`,
`tools/i18n-hardcoded-check.cjs` and the `i18n.test.ts` catalog-hygiene block all read the
**catalogs** and the **renderer**. A string that is born in `src/main/**` and travels to the screen
through a `message` / `error` / `detail` field passes all three, is in no catalog, and appears in
English no matter what language the user chose. **This is why D171 and D172 survived a full i18n
class pass that was marked `done`.**

**Claimed and released by primary2:** the nyaa/subtitle producers only (D171, D172). **The rest of
the list below is UNCLAIMED and is the next worker's work-list.** Split it by FILE, not by class —
that is what the last five collisions taught.

**The scan (reusable, and a LEAD not a verdict):** for each `setX(v.field)` in `src/renderer`,
walk back ≤25 lines to `v`'s assignment and check whether it is a `window.api.*` call.
**36 leads, 30 resolved to `window.api`, in 15 files:**

| file | sites | triaged? |
|------|-------|----------|
| `views/YouTubePlaylistsView.tsx` | **9** | **YES — real, D173 below** |
| `components/anki/DeckWorkbench.tsx` | 4 | no |
| `components/discover/SubtitleHarvestPanel.tsx` | 3 (fetch/attach paths) | no — D171 fixed only the LISTING half |
| `components/blanc/BlancReadyToolPanels.tsx` | 3 | no |
| `components/settings/pages/MalSyncPanel.tsx` | 2 (`.summary`) | no |
| `anki/DeckWorkbenchGlossary`, `DictionaryResults`, `discover/MalDownloadDialog`, `grammar/GrammarContent`, `immersion/ImmersionContent`, `media/library/NyaaSubtitleDialog` (accept path), `media/StudyOrchestratorWorkspace`, `music/MusicContent`, `SeanimeDevPanel` | 1 each | no |

**Triage rule, and it matters — do not file 30 rows.** A `.error` that is a caught exception
(`e.message`, a yt-dlp stderr line, an HTTP status) is technical text and showing it raw is
defensible. What is a defect is a **designed product refusal**: a sentence someone wrote for a
person to read, which therefore belongs in a catalog. Read the producer before filing.

## 2026-09-07 06:30 EDT — primary2 (worktree `wt/files-app`): D155 closed, and the class it opened

`primary` named D155 as the exact next slice and correctly refused it — all four catalog files
are dirty from another track in the MAIN tree, so a HEAD+edit blob would be silently dropped.
**They are clean in this worktree.** So this turn took it, and following `formatDuration` out to
its neighbours found three more of the same class.

### The class, stated once because it is the transferable part

**Translations that were written, translated into ja/zh/ru, and never wired to a consumer.**
Two whole key blocks were sitting in all four catalogs with **zero call sites**:

| block | keys | the component that should have used them |
|---|---|---|
| `mediaProfile.*` | 15 | `MediaLanguageProfileCard.tsx` — every string an English literal |
| `vnPanel.duration.*` | 2 | `VisualNovelPanel.tsx` — a local `formatDuration` with English literals ten lines away |

**No check in this repo could see it, and that is the point.** `i18n-check` asks whether a key is
TRANSLATED. `i18n-hardcoded-check` asks whether a file adopts `t()` **at all** — and the card
adopts nothing, so it is not even a partial-adoption case. `partial-i18n-scan.cjs` (D100) only
scores files that already call `t()`. A file with no `t()` and a complete translated key block of
its own is invisible to all three, and stays green forever.

The gate added here is a **usage** gate, and it earned itself on its first run: it immediately
flagged `mediaProfile.minutes` as having no consumer either. That key was **deleted from all four
catalogs** rather than wired to nothing — the card's duration line now goes through the shared
`formatDuration`, which speaks the language *and* handles hours, which that key never could.

### The worst shape of the four: English frozen into the user's data

`difficulty.recommendation` is not a render-time literal. `recommendation(knownRatio, level)`
(`mediaStudyWorkflow.ts:113`) builds an English sentence **at analysis time and stores it** in
`mediaStudyDatabase`. A language switch can never reach it, because by then it is data.

Fixed without a migration and without touching the schema: `recommendationKind(knownRatio)` is
now exported and the card re-derives the verdict at render from `knownRatio`, which is stored
right beside the sentence. **Profiles already in the user's store therefore translate too.** The
English field is still written, so any other consumer keeps working; the card no longer reads it.

### Mutation controls — six, all fire

| control | result |
|---|---|
| restore the bare `m` literal in `stats.formatDuration` | 4 fail |
| delete the **zh** minute key | 3 fail — the *English-fallback* shape, so the by-name catalog test is what catches it |
| leave one Latin `h` in the **ru** hours-and-minutes form | 2 fail |
| render `profile.difficulty.band` raw again | 3 fail, one of them the usage gate |
| print `profile.difficulty.recommendation` (the frozen sentence) again | 1 fail |
| unwire `vnPanel.duration.hm` back to a literal | 1 fail — **the usage gate found it from source text alone**, which is exactly how it would have caught D176 on the day the key landed |

## 2026-09-07 06:55 EDT — primary2: D177's general form is BUILT, and it found the class is much bigger

`tools/i18n-orphan-key-check.cjs` — every English key that **no source file ever asks for**, over
all 12,642 declared keys and every `.ts`/`.tsx` under `src/`. Ratcheted per namespace against
`tools/i18n-orphan-key-baseline.json`, not a hard zero, for a reason the first run proved.

### It was USELESS on its first run, and the correction is the transferable part

**1,763 orphans — of which 602 were `scrApp.*`, and every one of those is reached.**
`scraper/strings.ts` holds `const SHARED_PREFIX = 'scrApp.'` and composes `SHARED_PREFIX + key`
inside `sx()`. A scan that only credits an inline `'prefix.' +` or a `` `prefix.${` `` misses that
entirely — **a prefix held in a named constant is the normal way to write this**, not an exotic
case. The check now resolves `const X = 'a.b.'` bindings and credits them when `X +` or `${X}`
appears in the same file. That alone took the count from **1,763 to 1,077** without a line of
product change. A number this instrument produces is worth nothing until you have opened the
largest namespace it reports and confirmed the keys really are unreachable.

Also excluded, deliberately, and the reason is not the obvious one: **`__tests__` does not count
as a consumer.** A test naming a key does not make the product use it, and crediting it would let
a component be deleted while its keys still read as live.

### Why a baseline and not zero

1,077 remain and a large share are legitimate. `scraperPage.find.*` (189) is the clearest case:
those are search-keyword strings for settings cards that were **removed**, and the file's own
comment explains the ten that survive. Keys held for a surface not yet built are the same shape.
So the number that matters is not the total — it is **a namespace that goes from having a
consumer to having none**, which is the only shape that is always a defect. The ratchet catches
exactly that.

**Control:** deleting one `t('mediaAssistant.grammar')` call site makes the check exit 1 and name
the key — `REGRESSION mediaAssistant.grammar: 1 orphaned, baseline allows 0`. Without that, a
"nothing to do" reading would mean nothing.

`npx eslint tools/i18n-orphan-key-check.cjs` reports the same 2 `no-var-requires` errors that
`tools/i18n-locale-arg-check.cjs` reports; every `.cjs` in `tools/` does. Not new.

### 2026-09-07 07:15 EDT — primary2: the check's own first two false readings, and D181

Two more instrument corrections, both found by USING the check rather than by reading it, and
both of which had already produced a wrong answer:

**1. A comment is not a consumer.** `mediaLibActions.working` read as USED on the first baseline
because a doc comment in `.coordination/liquid-workplace/probes/l8-states-video.cjs` listed it as
an example. Every other key in that block was correctly reported dead, so **one comment was all
it took to keep a dead key alive indefinitely** — the exact failure `destructive-guard-scan.cjs`
shipped with and had to repair, now repeated in a second instrument. Comments are masked
length-preservingly; string bodies deliberately are **not**, because a key IS a string literal.
`.coordination/` is exempt entirely for the same reason `__tests__` is: measurement scaffolding
naming a key does not make the product render it.

That one change exposed **7 more genuinely dead keys** that had been credited by comments alone:
`settings.home.quick.memory`, `assetError.httpFailed`, `assetError.emptyResponse`,
`library.open`, and the four `verifiedSites.source.*` that became D181.

**2. A multi-line plural entry is not one line, and this one broke the branch.** Deleting the 16
`mediaLibActions.*` keys with a line filter anchored on `^\s+'mediaLibActions\.` removed the KEY
line of `removedCount` in `ru.ts` and left its four CLDR arms and closing brace dangling inside
the previous entry. `ru.ts` stopped parsing; esbuild said
`Expected identifier but found "'declension.title'"` — pointing at the **next** key, six lines
past the damage. Only ru had the multi-line form, so en/ja/zh parsed fine and a spot check would
have missed it. Repaired, and `i18n-check` re-run to zero (12,839 keys, all translated) and
`i18n-dupe-keys` to 10,872 in each of the four. **Run `i18n-check` after any scripted catalog
edit — it is the only thing here that actually parses the file.**

Continues the class the 06:30 section opened: **translations that were written, translated into
ja/zh/ru, and never wired to a consumer.** `primary2`'s previous turn found four such blocks;
this turn found the whole visual-novel surface was one, across **seven** files and **243** keys.

**Orphan keys app-wide: 1,066 → 823.** All seven `vn*` namespaces are now 0.

### What made it invisible, and it is worth repeating once

The panel that opened this — `VisualNovelPanel.tsx` — is the ONE file of the seven that already
called `t()` (17 times). That is what makes it the interesting case: it was PARTIALLY converted,
so `i18n-hardcoded-check` sees an adopting file and passes, `i18n-check` sees translated keys and
passes, and `i18n-partial-check` **ratchets** — a file that has always been bad stays baselined
at its own badness. Its baseline was **67**. Only `i18n-orphan-key-check.cjs`, added yesterday in
`d00b8c06`, could see it, and only from the CATALOG side.

The six siblings are the simpler shape: five of them called `t()` **zero** times.

### The defects

|---|---------|--------------|----------|-----|--------|

The catalogs already carried `vnImport.msg.exportedOk` with a comment naming this defect; nobody had wired the fix. | The key's own comment in `catalogs/en.ts`, plus the template literal at the export handler. | P2 | **fixed — `62a03d9f`.** Path checked before interpolation. |

### Two traps this turn, both cheap to inherit and expensive to rediscover

**1. A `not.toContain` on a string that never renders passes in every language and proves
nothing.** Three languages of negative assertions were green on `Local discovery` — which the
composed panel does not render AT ALL, because `ReadingCanvas` lays a tool out only after it
measures and jsdom measures 0, so the whole library rail is absent from the mount. The fix is
structural and belongs in any i18n render test: **assert the same list PRESENT in English
first**, in the same test file, from the same mount. `VisualNovelImportPanel` then needs its own
mount, which is also the only way to reach its `aria-label` — `textContent` cannot see one.

**2. The scope rule cuts both ways inside a single control, and the partial-check baseline is
where that gets recorded.** The engine picker's `Custom` and `Unknown` are chrome and translate;
`Ren'Py`, `KiriKiri`, `NScripter`, `Unity`, `RPG Maker`, `TyranoBuilder` are product names and
must not. Three of those are baselined in `tools/i18n-partial-baseline.json` as legitimately
literal — and a test asserts all six still RENDER in Russian, so the baseline cannot quietly
become cover for a real regression later.

All four translations happen to put `{when}` last today so the trim reads correctly and the English fallback never fires — but any translation that fronted the slot would have produced a leading particle, and the fallback is an untranslated literal in a fully-translated file. | `ResourcesView.tsx` refreshLabel, three pieces of string surgery on one key. | P3 | **fixed — this commit.** A dedicated `resources.updatedRecently` replaces the surgery, and the now-consumerless `resources.updated` was deleted from all four catalogs after confirming zero other call sites. |

**The transferable part: a shell behind a theme branch is a shell no test mounts.** `AppChrome`
is the app's one sanctioned structural theme branch, and `useAeroMaterials()` reads
`data-materials` off `<html>`. Every suite in this repo runs on the default material, so the
entire Aero half of every view that has one is unexercised. Reaching it costs one line —
`document.documentElement.setAttribute('data-materials', 'aero')` before the mount — and the new
suite asserts `.aero-resources-workbench` exists so it cannot silently fall back to scoring the
standard shell. **Other views with the same branch are worth the same check:** `CalendarView`
and `FlashcardsView` both build `MenuBarMenu[]`, and `FlashcardsView` already routes its labels
through `flash.aero.menu.*`, so `resources` may not be the only one.

**A trap that cost me a restore:** reverting a mutation with `git checkout -- <catalog>` **wiped
the whole new key block**, because the file also carried this turn's uncommitted work. Mutation
controls on a file you are mid-edit on must be restored from a `cp` backup taken before the
mutation, never from the index.

### D187's lead, followed: every view with an `AppChrome` theme branch, scanned

`i18n-partial-check --file` rather than guessing:

## 2026-09-07 15:35 EDT — primary, main tree: the automatic-subtitle chain, and one class three times

Claimed and closed a **cross-cutting thread rather than a surface**: the 09-07 09:55 pin's own
objective — *"make sure the automatic subtitle finding for downloaded episodes actually works"* —
measured hop by hop against the user's real `media.json` (39 items, 37 video, 11 with any subtitle
track). **No coverage cell moved and none should**; this is not a surface walk.

**D250, D251 and D252 are one defect three times, and it is worth naming as a class:**

> **A fixed rule does not repair what the old rule already wrote, and nothing revisits it.**

- **D250** — the release parser learned `-END`; both backfill sites gate on
  `seriesKey === undefined`, and a *wrong* key is not an absent one.
- **D251** — the sharpest. `subtitleDiscovery.ts:360` has refused this attachment since
  2026-08-24 **and its own comment cites the three still-attached bad rows as the measurement
  that produced the guard.** They were left in place, and the sweep's eligibility filter then
  made them unreachable: a wrong ja track satisfies "has the wanted language" as well as a
  right one.
- **D252** — the client exposes a `down` flag precisely so an outage is not reported as a fact
  about the show; `jimakuSearch` drops it, and `no-match` is evidential, so a 429 suppressed
  the provider for 7 days.

**The habit this argues for:** when you fix a rule that writes to a store, ask in the same commit
what the old rule already wrote — and *measure it against the real profile*. All three answers
came from bundling the shipping function with `esbuild` and running it over a **copy** of
`%APPDATA%\jp-study-app\media.json`. That took seconds, needed no app restart, and is a
repeatable recipe: `npx esbuild <module> --bundle --platform=node --format=cjs --outfile=<tmp>`,
then `require` it from a throwaway script under `~\.claude-runs\`.

**A trap that cost a re-run, worth more than the fix:** three `perl -0pi -e` mutation controls
read as clean passes because the pattern ended in `\n` and the file is CRLF — the substitution
silently matched nothing. **Verify a mutation APPLIED before you believe it passed**: line count,
occurrence count, or a grep for the mutated text. Re-run with `\r?\n`, all three fired.

**Left open, deliberately, and it is the next slice:** D252's *record* half is source-verified,
not driven. `discoverForItem` is not in `__subtitleDiscoveryTestables` and no harness drives
`runSubtitleDiscovery` with a mocked provider client. Build one — electron is already mocked in
`src/main/__tests__/subtitleDiscovery.test.ts` — and it closes three things at once: D252's
record half, D251's prune firing inside the sweep, and the **nyaa and opensubtitles arms, which
still collapse an outage into `no-match` and are NOT fixed here.**

**Also measured, not filed as a defect, so the next worker does not re-derive it:** of the user's
29 Big O items, 24 have no subtitle at all (episodes 2–12, 14–26), and those 24 are exactly the

**The class, now five deep in one chain: D252 (jimaku) → D253 (opensubtitles) → D254 (nyaa
config) → D255/D256 (what gets written and kept) → D257 (metadata).** Every one is the same
sentence: *an empty result was recorded as a fact about the content, and the record then
suppressed the retry that would have corrected it.* Twice the correct contract already existed
one layer down and a convenience wrapper threw it away (`jimakuSearch`, `openSubtitlesSearch`),
and once a comment asserted the collapse was harmless while the caller made it permanent
(`searchProviders`). **The generalisable check, for any provider call anywhere in this repo:
follow the empty result to whatever writes it down, and ask what re-asks.**

and both keep the distinction — they report it as `provenance.failures` / `servedBy: null`
rather than collapsing it. More importantly they **persist nothing**, so there is no stored
record to suppress a later retry, which is the half that makes this class expensive. A defect
here would have been cosmetic; there isn't one. `browseDiscovery` is over-inclusive rather than
under (an empty-but-answered Jikan is pushed as a `failure`), which is the safe direction.

## 2026-09-07 16:20 EDT — primary2 (worktree `wt/files-app`): the same class, one surface over

The handoff's carry-forward was: **follow any empty provider result to whatever writes it down, and ask
what re-asks.** Applied to `youtube`, which no worker had read on the main-process side. Five findings,
four fixed in `c79adcc7`.

**The strongest one is not that class at all, and it is worth the sentence.** `yt:autoUpdateDue` had **no
caller** — handled in main, bound in `preload.ts:1887`, declared in `window.d.ts:1131`, invoked nowhere in
`src/renderer`. So the "Update automatically" checkbox and its 6/12/24-hour select persisted a preference
that nothing read. **And underneath it the sweep's own filter was `due && !due`** and would have selected
no playlist even if something had called it: `normalizeYtStore` copies a playlist's `lastCheckedAt` and
`updateFrequencyHours` onto its channel on every read, so the "skip playlists whose channel is due" clause
was the negation of the due test beside it. Two independent reasons the feature could never work, stacked.

**The check that generalises, and it is cheap:** `grep -rn <apiName> src/renderer` for every entry in
`preload.ts`. A binding whose only hits are `window.d.ts` and `preload.ts` is a feature with no actor. The
pin's class 6 asks whether a *setting* is read; this asks whether a *route* is called, and it is the same
defect one layer up. `route-with-no-consumer` is already a known shape in this repo — it has now cost a
whole shipped feature.

**Second, from the instrument: a test written against the intent found the filter bug; a test written
against a fixture would not have.** The `runAutoUpdateDue` cases build a real store on disk and let
`readStore()` run the real `normalizeYtStore`. Two of the fourteen failed on first run, and both failures
were the product, not the test — the normalizer was rewriting the field the assertions were about. Had the
suite stubbed the store shape, both defects would have passed straight through.

**Verified before filing rather than assumed:** the live store (`%APPDATA%\jp-study-app\yt-playlists.json`,
19 videos / 2 playlists / 1 channel) has **0 rows with `title === youtubeId`**, so D241 is latent on this
profile. And `diffNewVideos` was checked against the same class and is **CLEAN** — it keys off
`firstSeenAt`, stamped at merge time, so a failed playlist's new videos are still reported after
`yt:refreshAll` advances `lastNewsCheckedAt`.

**Erratum:** `c79adcc7`'s message calls the self-negating filter **D239**. That id was already taken (the
Settings menu-bar row). Its id is **D244**; the register row says so.

Also note `ImmersionDayMetrics` declares five counters and only two are ever bumped — `seconds`, `chars` and `wordsMined` are written by nobody either. | P3 | **open, and deliberately not fixed this turn — the fix is a product decision, not a repair.** Two honest resolutions and they go opposite ways: surface the metrics (a new panel — by the pin's own test that is a *wish*, since immersion does not fail its stated purpose without a daily counter) or stop writing them. The session half is the stronger of the two and points the other way: a browser that loses every tab on restart while carrying a complete, tested restore path is a missing connection, not a wish. Whoever takes it should take the **session** half first and leave the metrics alone until something asks to read them. Do not "fix" this by deleting the main-process handlers — that throws away the half that is already correct. |

**Why this one is worth a row when six siblings are not:** class 6b asks whether a route has a
caller. All eight of these have none. Seven are *symmetric* — no writer and no reader, so
nothing accumulates. `immersionBumpMetrics` is **asymmetric**: the write path is live and
shipping, the read path is dead, so the store has been filling up with the user's activity since
the surface shipped and no code path can ever show it back to them. **An unread store is worse
than an uncalled route, and the two look identical to a caller scan.**
