built, tested and correct, and the thing feeding it was never connected. D317's `MediaStatusPill`
had a working component and a `null` argument; D318's `resolveSeasonForEpisode` has a full unit
suite and a field that is empty on 39 of 39 live items. Both read as "no defect" to a source scan
and to the test suite, and both are obvious the moment you look at the running app with the
user's own data. **A green suite over a dead input is the failure mode of this whole track.**

Defect ids: **D317–D318** from `primary`'s range 310–329. **319–329 remain.**

## 2026-09-08 — `primary`, class 4 (count-vs-truth) by hand, surface `scraper`

| # | surface | what the USER sees | repro (exact) | sev | status |
|---|---------|--------------------|---------------|-----|--------|
| D319 | scraper — Dashboard "Runtime" card vs the rail's STATUS block | **The same window states two different values for the same number, at the same instant.** The left rail says `Memory: 845 MB` and `CPU: 6%`; the Runtime card a few inches to its right says `1672` MB and `22%` CPU. Nothing on screen says one is older than the other, so whichever the user believes, they are reading a figure that has not been true for however long the window has been open. | Open Scraper → Dashboard. Read the STATUS block at the bottom of the left rail and the "Runtime" card in the same pass. Measured live 2026-09-08 03:49 UTC (pid 4652, window 1) in **one** `/eval` read so the two cannot be separated in time: rail `["Memory: 845 MB","CPU: 6%"]`, card `["1672 Memory (MB)","22% CPU","0 Active jobs"]`. Leave the window open a few minutes and the gap widens — the rail moves, the card never does. | P2 | **fixed `3d841f96`** |

**Cause, and it is the class-4 shape exactly: one quantity, two scopes.** `ScraperApp` polls
`port.systemStats()` every 3 s into shell state and hands it to the rail. `DashboardPage` took its
*own* one-shot `port.systemStats()` inside the `Promise.all` that builds its once-per-mount
snapshot (`useEffect(..., [port])`, and `port` never changes), stored it as `DashboardSnapshot.stats`,
and rendered the card from that. So the card is a photograph of the moment the page was opened,
hung next to a live meter.

**Fix.** The shell composes the reading once — `{...stats, activeJobs: activeJobCount}`, which is
already what the rail gets — and publishes it as `ScraperController.systemStats`. The card reads
`ctl.systemStats`. `stats` is **removed from `DashboardSnapshot` entirely**, along with the port
call that filled it, so there is no second copy left to drift: a volatile reading cannot live in a
structure that is read once per mount.

**After, measured the same way, one read:** rail `["Memory: 1,098 MB","CPU: 5%"]`, card
`["1098 Memory (MB)","5% CPU","0 Active jobs"]` — and the 845→1,098 move between the two readings
is itself the proof the card now tracks rather than freezes.

**Regression test** `src/renderer/__tests__/scraperDashboardRuntimeLive.test.tsx` (3 cases). Its
control is `createMockScraperPort().systemStats()`, which answers a fixed `{152, 2, 1}` — the value
a revived one-shot read would put on screen, distinct from everything the test supplies. **Mutation
run: restoring the one-shot read failed 2 of 3, rendering exactly `152 / 2% / 1`.**

Defect ids: **D319** from `primary`'s range 310–329. **320–329 remain.**

| # | surface | what the USER sees | repro (exact) | sev | status |
|---|---------|--------------------|---------------|-----|--------|
| D320 | scraper — Dashboard "Episodes indexed", in three places at once | **The Dashboard says the scraper has indexed 2,453 episodes. It has indexed 1,193.** Scrape a series a second time and every episode it holds is counted again, so the headline number grows by re-running work rather than by finding anything new. On this profile it reads **2.06× the truth**, and the same inflated figure appears in the hero strip, the `EPISODES INDEXED` tile, and the "Study handoff" card — three agreeing places, all wrong together, which is exactly what makes it believable. | Open Scraper → Dashboard and read any of the three. Measured live 2026-09-08 (pid 4652, window 1) straight off `scraperListJobs` + `scraperGetResult`: **10 jobs, 5 distinct series, 2,453 episode rows, 1,193 distinct episodes.** ONE PIECE was scraped **twice** (1,147 rows each — and 1,147 of 1,147 `EpisodeRow.id`s identical between the two runs) and Frieren **five times** (28 rows each). 1,147 + 28 + 12 + 4 + 3 = 1,194 by series, 1,193 measured by id. | P2 | **fixed `1159f5fc`** |

**Cause — class 4 in its purest form: the predicate that COUNTS and the predicate that LABELS
are at different scopes.** `DashboardPage` flattened `result.episodes` across jobs and took
`.length`. That is a count of *rows returned by runs*; the label says *episodes indexed*. The two
agree only on an install that never re-scraped anything.

**This exact defect was already found and fixed one surface over.** `scraperDashboardResultLink.test.ts`'s
own header records it: the Results library moved to a per-job id "during the Phase-4 fix that
stopped five runs of one series reading as 500% catalogue coverage". The Dashboard's own totals
were never moved with it. Five runs of Frieren is *literally the same data* that produced that
earlier bug.

**Fix.** `distinctEpisodes(rows)` in `dashboardData.ts` keys on `EpisodeRow.id`, which is derived
from the series and episode number (`anilist-21-e1`) and is stable across runs — verified above at
1,147/1,147. It returns the Japanese-track count deduplicated on the same identity, so that figure
cannot drift from the one beside it. A row with no id counts once on its own terms rather than
collapsing every unidentifiable row into one.

**After, live, same three places, after a navigate-away-and-back to force a fresh snapshot:**
hero `1,193 indexed episodes`, tile `Episodes indexed 1,193`, handoff `1,193 Episodes indexed` —
matching the 1,193 measured independently from the raw IPC before any code changed.

**Mutations, both caught:** (a) collapsing the id-less fallback to a constant → the id-less control
fails; (b) swapping the two `Set`s back to arrays, i.e. counting rows again → the two dedupe cases
fail. 5 new cases in `dashboardData.test.ts`, 22 green.

Defect ids: **D320** from `primary`'s range 310–329. **321–329 remain.**

| # | surface | what the USER sees | repro (exact) | sev | status |
|---|---------|--------------------|---------------|-----|--------|
| D321 | scraper — History, the tile row and the jobs grid | **Two numbers side by side in two different formats.** The tile row reads `EPISODES FOUND 2,453` next to `FAILED CHECKS 2156`; in the jobs grid the ONE PIECE row reads `1,147` under FOUND and `1078` under FAILED, in adjacent columns. It reads like `1078` is a different kind of quantity, or like one of them is a code rather than a count. | Open Scraper → History. Read the four tiles, then the FOUND and FAILED columns of the ONE PIECE row. Seen live 2026-09-08 (pid 4652, window 1). Any job with four or more digits of failures shows it; on this profile ONE PIECE has 1,078, twice. | P3 | **fixed `8e67251e`** |

**Cause.** Two call sites in `DataPages.tsx`: `totals.episodes.toLocaleString()` and
`job.found.toLocaleString()` group; `{totals.failures}` and `{job.failed}` interpolate the raw
number. Both fixed. **Live after: `2,156` and `1,078`.**

**Test** `src/renderer/__tests__/scraperHistoryNumberGrouping.test.tsx` asserts the *contract* —
every job's FOUND and FAILED cell equals that value's own `toLocaleString()` — so it holds at any
magnitude and in any locale rather than pinning today's digits. Its fixture carries the user's real
`found 1,147 / failed 1,078` deliberately: **under 1,000 the two formats are identical and the
regression passes unseen**, which is why this survived every previous pass over this page. Mutation
reverting both call sites fails both cases.

**Checked and NOT filed on the same page**, so the next worker does not re-derive them: `FAILED
CHECKS` 2,156 = 1,078 × 2 ✓; `AVERAGE RUNTIME 3s` = 32 s / 10 jobs ✓; `All jobs 10 · Problems 2 ·
Clean runs 8` matches `jobOutcome` (found > 0 with failures = warning) ✓; `DATA INDEXED 0 MB` — all
ten jobs carry `bytes: 0` ✓. `EPISODES FOUND 2,453` is the row sum and is left alone **here**: this
page is explicitly about runs and labels its own chart "Episodes found per run". D320 is about the
Dashboard, which is not.

Defect ids: **D321** from `primary`'s range 310–329. **322–329 remain.**

| # | surface | what the USER sees | repro (exact) | sev | status |
|---|---------|--------------------|---------------|-----|--------|
| D322 | scraper — Results page tiles, and the Dashboard beside it | **Two pages of the same app disagree about how big the library is.** Results says `SERIES 10` and `10 series`; the Dashboard's own tile says `SERIES 5`. Results also says `EPISODES 2,453` where the catalogue holds 1,193. And the page proves itself wrong in its own list: Frieren appears as **five identical cards** and ONE PIECE as **two**, so a user counting the cards gets 10 and counting the shows gets 5. | Open Scraper → Results, read the four tiles and the `N series` line, then open Dashboard and read its `SERIES` tile. Measured live 2026-09-08 (pid 4652, window 1). | P2 | **fixed `ff8d2804`** |

**Cause — and the interesting part is that the underlying behaviour is CORRECT.** `librarySeriesId`
is job-scoped, so one run is one library row; that is deliberate and is exactly what stopped five
proof runs of Frieren reading as "500% catalogue coverage" (`resultLibrary.ts` says so in its own
header). What was never updated is what the page *calls* those rows, and what it sums over them.
`bySeries.length` is a count of runs under a label that says Series; `rows.length` is a count of
episode rows under a label that says Episodes.

**Fix — a label plus a deduplicated total, NOT a regrouping.** `results.series` → `results.count`
("Results") and the `N series` header → `results.countLabel`, in `strings.ts` and in all four
`scraperUi` catalogs (en/ja/zh/ru, with the ru plural forms). EPISODES and WITH JAPANESE SUBTITLES
now go through `distinctEpisodes()` keyed on `EpisodeRow.id`, which `buildResultLibrary` leaves
alone (it re-tags only `seriesId`). The FAILED tile picked up the grouping its neighbours had.

**Live after:** `RESULTS 10`, `EPISODES 1,193`, `10 results` — and 1,193 is the first time this page
and the Dashboard have agreed. `node tools/i18n-check.cjs` exit 0, 12,940 keys.

**Deliberately NOT changed, recorded so it is not "fixed" later by accident:** the cards stay
per-run. Merging Frieren's five cards into one would be the nicer page, and it would also undo the
job-scoped coverage fix — the per-card `own.length / expected` reading is only meaningful per run.
If a future turn wants one card per series, it must re-derive coverage at the series level first.
The page subtitle "grouped by series" is left alone for the same reason.

**Mutation:** restoring `rows.length` and the `Series` label fails both cases in
`src/renderer/__tests__/scraperResultsTotals.test.tsx`.

Defect ids: **D319–D322** from `primary`'s range 310–329. **323–329 remain.**

| D334 | youtube | **Work you do while YouTube is quietly updating itself is thrown away.** The app checks tracked playlists for new videos on its own, on an hourly clock nobody starts and nothing on screen mentions. The store is written whole: `ytPlaylists.ts:129-143` `writeStore` serialises the entire object, and every long writer read a snapshot, awaited a provider, then wrote that snapshot back (`runAutoUpdateDue` at :563-574 being the unattended one, `main.ts:1764` starting its timer). Before the fix the folder list becomes `[]`, `autoUpdate` returns to `true`, and the deleted playlist reappears. | P0 | **fixed `0691b761`** — `commitStore()` is now the only write path for anything that awaits: it re-reads the store at commit time and applies a *synchronous* mutator, so nothing can interleave with it. `syncPlaylistFromUrl` splits into `fetchPlaylist` (network, holds no store) and `applyPlaylistSync` (sync, inside the commit, re-resolving the playlist **by id** — which is what makes a mid-sync rename or `autoUpdate:false` survive, since `{...existing}` then spreads the user's *current* values). A mutator returning `null` aborts the write entirely: that is how a response already in flight declines to resurrect something the user deleted. **Applied to all eight windows, not only the timer** — `runAutoUpdateDue`, `addPlaylistByUrl`, `addVideoByUrl`, `yt:addPlaylist`, `yt:refreshPlaylist`, `yt:refreshChannel`, `yt:refreshAll`, `downloadVideosByIds`, `yt:fetchSubsOnly`; guarding timer-versus-timer overlap alone would have left the seven user-driven ones open, and a Refresh all is just as long. The nine synchronous handlers were checked and left alone: they read and write with no await between, so they are already atomic. Gate: `ytStoreLostUpdate.test.ts`, 10 cases, whose instrument is a provider that parks until the test releases it — without that a sweep completes before the next statement and the race is unreachable. **Mutation control run for real:** restoring the one-snapshot sweep turns exactly **3 of 10 red** by name, and the positive control (a sweep with no concurrent write still lands its videos) stays green, so the fix is not "never write anything". |

Source: `.rf-drawer-layer` is `position: fixed; inset: 0; z-index: 60` and `.rf-drawer-scrim` is `rgb(0 0 0 / 0.28)` with an `onClick` (`readingSiteDrawer.css:19-33`); the `<aside role="dialog">` had no `aria-modal`, no Tab handling anywhere in the file, and Escape on its own `onKeyDown` (`ReadingFinderContent.tsx:512`). | P2 | **fixed `de275196`** — replaced the hand-rolled focus effect with `ui/useModalKeyboard` (Escape at the document, focus-in, Tab trap, focus-restore) and added `aria-modal="true"`. 7/7, the 4 pre-existing cases unchanged. **The scrim sweep's file list is now exhausted, and six of its seven were false leads.** The last
handoff listed `MiniShell`, `lens/LensReadPanel`, `media/StudyToolSheet`, `MangaHandwritingPopup`,
`ui/Window`, `reading-garden/ReadingGarden` and `ReadingFinderContent` as unswept for the
"backdrop dims + blocks ⇒ owes `aria-modal`" shape. Applying the discriminator rather than the
`aria-modal=0` count: **all six of the first are `role="dialog"` on a POSITIONED element**
(`left/top/width/height`), i.e. panels and floating windows, with no full-viewport backdrop in the
file at all. `MiniShell`'s `mini-backdrop` is an `aria-hidden` wallpaper, not a scrim. They are
correct as they are and must not be "fixed" — same conclusion the earlier turn reached for
`FieldHint`/`QuickSettings`/`NotificationCenter`.

**The lesson worth keeping: `aria-modal=0` is a LEAD, not a finding.** Six of seven files that
counted zero were right to. The thing that separates them is one CSS lookup — does the backdrop
paint a colour and swallow clicks — and the file that failed it was the only one whose backdrop was
`position: fixed`. Grep the CSS, not the JSX.

### 2026-09-08 06:05 EDT — primary, `agent`: the answer the user reads is raw markdown

| # | surface | what the USER sees | repro (exact) | sev | status |
|---|---------|--------------------|---------------|-----|--------|
| D391 | agent, blanc | **Every assistant answer is shown as raw markdown.** Asking the Agent a question returns text like `The Japanese verb **「食べる」** means **"to eat"**` and `- *私たちはご飯を食べます.*` — the asterisks, the backticks, the `###` and the `1.` are all rendered as literal characters, on the app's headline AI surface and in Blanc's central agent panel. Both providers and the local Qwen host answer in markdown whether or not they were asked to, so this is what a user sees on *every* answer, not an edge case. | Live, window 2, pid 4652: opened `agent`, created a conversation, sent "What does 食べる mean?" through the default **Local Qwen** provider. Streamed and completed correctly (`Answered on this device`), and the DOM was `<p class="agent-message-text">` holding the model's markdown verbatim — `w.querySelector(".agent-message-text").innerHTML` contained `**「食べる」**` with no `<strong>` anywhere; `strong,em,code,li` inside the whole window: **7**, all of them chrome, **0** in the message body. `AgentWorkspaceShell.tsx:695` and `BlancCentralAgentPanel.tsx:458` both interpolated `{message.text}` straight into a `<p>`. | P2 | **fixed `e0ef75f4`** — verified live on the same stored answer after HMR: 6 `<strong>`, a real `<ul>` with 4 `<li>`, and the user's own prompt still a plain `<p class="agent-message-text">`. |

**The fix is a parser, not a `String.replace`, and that is the security half.** `src/shared/agentMarkdown.ts`
turns an answer into a data tree of blocks and spans; `AgentMessageText.tsx` maps that tree to React
elements. Nothing in the path produces an HTML string, so provider output cannot become markup — the
test asserts `<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>` survives as *text*. Three further deliberate limits, each
a property rather than an omission:

- **A link is not an anchor.** `[label](url)` renders as `label (url)` in plain text. A model-supplied
  URL must not become one-click navigation out of a chat bubble; it stays readable and copyable.
- **An unclosed delimiter stays literal.** Answers render *while they stream*, so a half-arrived
  `**bold` must not swallow the remainder and re-flow when the pair lands.
- **A user message is never formatted.** What the user typed is what the user sees, asterisks included.
  `markdown={message.role === 'assistant'}` is the whole switch.

Headings render as `<h4 data-level>` rather than `<h1>`–`<h6>`: these sit inside a message list, and
injecting a document outline that competes with the surface's own headings would trade one
accessibility defect for another.

**Four mutation controls, all four fire** (`src/shared/__tests__/agentMarkdown.test.ts`, 13 cases):
an unclosed delimiter closing at end of line · the intraword-underscore guard removed so
`some_var_name` becomes two italic runs · a link dropping its URL · the heading pattern disabled.
Two earlier attempts did **not** fire and are worth naming: `close > contentFrom` → `close >= contentFrom`
is neutralised because the miss value is `-1`, and deleting the empty-blocks fallback changes nothing
because that line is unreachable against today's grammar. Both were replaced rather than reported —
a mutation that cannot reach the rule proves nothing about the test.

**Not a defect, checked and cleared, so nobody re-files it:** the navigate card's
`Review destination → Open Dictionary → "Opened Dictionary"` looked like a false success because no
`.fwin` appeared on either desk. It is true — `openAgentNavigationDestination` (`main.ts:1603`) opens a
**native pop-out `BrowserWindow`**, which `/health` showed as window id 3, `?popout=dictionary`. A
`.fwin` walk cannot see a pop-out. Closed it again; both desks ended as found.

chips, Covers view, Sort=Newest.

**There is no way to rename a library folder** — no `renameFolder` anywhere in the file, and the only
folder commands are new and delete. A typo therefore costs the filing of every book in that folder,
because delete unfiles them all. Filed as **D394, P3, open**: it is a missing capability rather than
a broken one, and adding a rename means a new editor state, a collision check against existing
folder names, a `setItemFolder` migration for every member, and four catalog keys — more than a
pre-sweep slice, and named here rather than half-built.

**The trap this turn nearly published, and it is the general one:** the navigate card and the folder
sort both *looked* broken through a probe that was asking the wrong object. `Opened Dictionary` with
no new `.fwin` is a pop-out `BrowserWindow`, and a `Length` sort that returns store order is either
a dead comparator or a shelf with no lengths — only reading the field on the live items separates
them. **Before filing "this control does nothing", read the datum it sorts, counts or filters on.**

**One instrument correction, because it produced a false clean bill in this very turn.** My first
inventory used `textContent` as the accessible name for every element and reported **0 unnamed on
every page**. `textContent` is not the accessible name of a `<select>` (it is the option list), nor
of a `<textarea>` (it is the value) — computing it that way *invents* a name for exactly the
controls most likely to lack one. Rewritten to follow HTML-AAM (`aria-labelledby`, then
`aria-label`, then `label[for]` / a wrapping `<label>`, then `value` for button-inputs, then
`placeholder`, then `title`), the same sweep found **10** unnamed controls. **Any earlier
"0 unnamed" in this register that was computed from textContent should be re-derived.**

**A second trap, and the reason the first five pages read `controls: 0`:** the Scraper restores its
Advanced Settings drawer open, and `scraper.css:5107` deliberately sets `.scr-main { display: none }`
while a drawer is open below a 1100px container. That is correct, documented behaviour — but it
means a page sweep must close the drawer first or every page measures empty. Also: match rail
buttons with `.scr-rail button`, not `w.querySelectorAll('button')` — the drawer's category list
carries the same labels ("Sources", "Torrents", "Profiles") and a whole-window match hits it.

Class 4 (count-vs-truth), found by asking the pin's question of every number on the Plugins page:
does the data agree? One did not, and it is the one the page exists to be trusted about.

| # | surface | what the USER sees | repro (exact) | sev | status |

| D418 | scraper - Plugins | **The Permission audit says no installed adapter wants anything.** `Jimaku Bridge` lists `http` and `subtitles` on its own card, and the panel immediately below it reads `0 Network access · 0 Browser control · 0 Storage access · 0 Broad access`. Under that sits a sentence about `Legacy AniX` — an adapter that is not installed and never was on this machine. | Live, pid 11736, window 2, Scraper - Plugins, on the app's real plugin store (2 installed: `broken-one`, whose manifest does not parse, and `Jimaku Bridge`). The audit's four predicates tested `permission.startsWith('network:'/'browser:'/'storage:')`, which is the vocabulary of the renderer's own `data/fixtures.ts`. The manifest reader validates against a different one — `main/scraper/plugins.ts` `KNOWN_PERMISSIONS` is `http, sources, torrents, metadata, subtitles, export, settings`, bare words — and anything carrying one of those prefixes falls into `unknownPermissions`, which sets `compatible: false`. **So the audit could only ever count adapters the app had already rejected: for every plugin that works, all four counters were structurally 0, on every install, forever.** Nothing else in the tree interprets these permissions, so the two lists had no third party to keep them honest. | P2 | **fixed — this commit.** The vocabulary and its groups now live together in `shared/scraperPluginPermissions.ts`; `main/scraper/plugins.ts` imports and re-exports `KNOWN_PERMISSIONS` from there, and the renderer asks the same module. Live after the fix: **Network access 0 -> 1**, and the negative control holds — `broken-one` declares no permissions and is not counted, so it reads 1 and not 2. The AniX sentence is replaced by a count-bearing one that renders only when a broad-access adapter really is installed; on this machine it now reads *"No installed adapter asks for wildcard network or direct filesystem access."* Gate `shared/__tests__/scraperPluginPermissions.test.ts`, 5 cases, one of which encodes the live Jimaku manifest and another of which asserts that `network` and `storage` are **reachable from the accepted vocabulary at all** — that last one is the shape of the defect rather than an instance of it. Mutation (point the resolver back at the `network:` prefix): **3 of 5 red**, and the two legacy-prefix cases correctly stay green because they test the other half. |

**Decision recorded, under the standing auto-approval for reversible internal choices.** The
permission-to-group map had to be invented — nothing in the tree defined one. `http`, `sources`,
`torrents`, `metadata` and `subtitles` are all **network**: each exists so an adapter can reach
something off this machine, which is what a reader of "Network access" wants to know. `export` and
`settings` are **storage**: they write things the user keeps. **`browser` deliberately has no
member** — this build has no browser-automation capability, so a 0 in that tile is the true answer
rather than a missing one, and a test asserts that emptiness on purpose so the next worker either
removes the tile or adds the capability instead of quietly mapping something else into it. The
legacy prefixed vocabulary is still recognised, because it only ever appears on a plugin this build
calls incompatible, and that is exactly the adapter whose reach the audit most needs to show.

**Cleared without a row on the same page, so they are not re-derived:** `Installed 2 · Enabled 0 ·
Updates 0 · Incompatible 1` all agree with the store (`broken-one` is the incompatible one, and its
card says why in plain words: *"The manifest is missing or could not be read."*); `updateAvailable`
is an empty string by design, with a comment saying an update check would mean a network call per
plugin per page visit; and the live log's four `WARN | plugins | broken-one: Expected property name
or '}' in JSON at position 2` lines are that same fixture being honest, not a failure.

**Also seen and NOT filed, with the reason.** The Selector Tester surfaces the browser's raw
exception verbatim — `Failed to execute 'querySelectorAll' on 'Document': '>>>bad<<<' is not a
valid selector.` — and it stays English in a Japanese UI. It is an engine message on a page whose
entire purpose is writing selectors, and the exact text is the diagnostic the user came for.
Translating it is not possible and paraphrasing it would cost information. Driven for real:
`.ep-list > li` -> 3 matches, `.ep-link` -> 3 `a.ep-link` matches with the right episode titles,
`>>>bad<<<` -> the message above with the card correctly reading `0 matches`.

**One behaviour worth knowing before the next scraper turn:** opening **Torrent Manager** runs an
indexer search by itself. Navigating to it during this sweep put `INFO | torrents | Nyaa (torrent
index): 75 results.` / `60 results after filters.` on the live log three times without a click. The
pin's "do not run the indexer search" limit cannot be honoured by not clicking — it is honoured by
not opening that page.

The clock had simply crossed the fixture's hardcoded instant about 90 minutes earlier. | P2 | **fixed — this commit.** `vi.useFakeTimers()` + `setSystemTime(NOW)` around the suite. Freezing is the right fix rather than making the fixture relative to `Date.now()`: `addedAt` and `lastReviewedAt` are also expressed against `NOW`, so one pinned instant keeps the whole deck coherent and the suite says the same thing next year as today. 9/9. The mutation control is the defect itself — removing the `beforeAll` reproduces the exact failure on today's date, which is how it was found. |

**LEAD CLOSED 2026-09-08 12:50 by `primary2` (`b2c50c80`), and the answer is the reassuring one:**
of **318** suites carrying a hardcoded calendar date — not the fifteen below, the whole class —
**4,618 tests, ZERO real time bombs** under a clock advanced 400 days. D419 was the only one. The
instrument, its positive control and its one blind spot are the class-8 row in the coverage table.
The original lead is kept below because its reasoning about the dangerous SHAPE is what made the
sweep cheap to scope.

**LEAD for the next worker, not a defect row — this class is almost certainly not alone.** Fifteen
suites hardcode a `Date.UTC(202x, …)` epoch and never pin the clock:

    renderer/__tests__/  deckScheduleSweeps · deckWorkbenchStale · flashcardDeckSrs ·
                         mediaHubStoragePanel · readingListTimelinePanel · schedulingPanel
    shared/__tests__/    agentSpendLedger · ankiLocalDeck · blancConsole · flashcardScheduling ·
                         localAgentAutomationRuns · localSrs · readingListExport ·
                         readingListTimeline · youtubeDiscovery

**Most are probably fine** — a suite that passes its own `now` into the subject is deterministic no
matter what the wall clock says, and several of these plainly do. The dangerous shape is narrower
and worth naming precisely: a fixture that computes a *relative* offset from a *fixed* epoch
(`EPOCH + 86_400_000`) and then calls a function whose `now` **defaults** to `Date.now()`. That
combination is true only until the wall clock overtakes the offset, and then it fails on a date
nobody chose, on a commit nobody made.

**How to settle it cheaply, without reading fifteen files:** run the suite under an advanced clock
and see which ones change answer. `npx vitest run <files>` with `TZ` untouched but the system time
moved a year forward is the decisive control — anything that flips was a time bomb, anything that
does not is genuinely deterministic. A green run today proves nothing about tomorrow, which is the
whole reason D419 reached the branch.

### 2026-09-08 12:35 EDT — primary2, files: D420 and D422 both CLOSED, and the order mattered

Live, pid 11736, **window 2**, on the user's own **43,686-item** index, served from this worktree,
after a full reload so the running module was really the new one (`hasReceipts` / `hasAnnounce`
both true in the served source; `VITE-ERROR-OVERLAY` count **0**).

**The mechanism was NOT what D422 guessed, and the correction is the useful part.** D422 recorded
"`bulkMineState` is component state and the refresh path resets it". That is false — nothing on the
refresh path touches it, proven by driving Refresh with a row selected and watching the selection
survive. The real cause is one line: `selected` is `visible.find(...)`, i.e. resolved from the
**filtered** list, and both receipts rendered *inside* the inspector that `selected` gates. So any
change that takes the mined row out of the current folder unmounts the receipt — and since D421
taught `Text not yet mined` to shrink, **that is the normal outcome of mining from it**, not an
unlucky one. Reproduced without any write at all: with a Note selected, clicking the `Video` rail
node dropped `selected` to null while `selectedId` was untouched.

**Which makes the fix order load-bearing, exactly as the D422 row predicted.** Fixing D420 first
would have turned a likely loss into a certain one: the auto-refresh removes the mined row from the
folder every time. Measured after the fix, the inspector was **already gone** at the moment the
receipt was read (`itemInspectorUp: false`, `selectedRow: no`) and the Undo was still there.

**Both fixes are recorded on the original D420 and D422 rows above, not repeated here** — the
register is one row per defect id and `presweepRegisterGate` enforces it.

**Three mutation controls, each red on exactly its own case with the others green:** receipts put
back inside the inspector → the D422 case fails by name; the bulk announce removed → the D420
positive fails; the announce made unconditional → the D420 *refusal* control fails. That third one
is why the refusal case is a separate test rather than an `expect` inside the first.

**A trap worth the two calls it cost.** `.fa-details` and `.fa-details-list` are BOTH reused by the
"nothing selected" state, so neither proves the item inspector is mounted — a test asserting the
inspector had closed passed while it was still open. `.fa-action-open` is the honest probe: it
exists only for a selected item.

**Residual, stated rather than hidden.** Selecting a different row still clears a *single*-item
mine receipt (`selectItem` resets `mineState` by design — "a new selection invalidates the previous
item's result"). That is a deliberate, documented invalidation the user drives themselves, not the
silent destruction D422 was about; it is listed here so it is not re-filed as the same defect.

(class 5, clean) — it does not name the item, which is a wording lead and not filed as a defect.

**A LEAD, for whoever runs the keyboard class next.** The three above had *nothing*. A wider scan
found **11 files that declare `aria-modal` and have no focus trap** —
`BlancMasterSearch`, `BlancShell`, `Lockscreen`, `TourOverlay`, `MangaProviderBrowser`,
`CssPlayground`, `WiredBreachOverlay`, `ui/Sheet`, `NovelReader`, `MediaWorkspaceHost`, and the
dev harness. **Nine of the ten real ones already handle Escape themselves**, and only
`BlancMasterSearch` / `BlancShell` trap Tab, so this is the *trap* half only and it is lower
severity than D423 — `Lockscreen` is deliberately not Escape-dismissible and must stay that way.
Do not batch-apply the hook to that list without reading each one.

### 2026-09-08 13:25 EDT — primary, agent: the language switch, and the surface closes

Live, **pid 22788, window 1**, on the user's own Agent workspace (4 conversations, **6 shelf items**).
The app's own control was used — Settings ▸ Appearance ▸ 日本語 — not a dynamically imported
`setUiLang`, which switches a DUPLICATE module instance and leaves the app in English (that trap is
already on record and it is why this pass had never been run here).

| id | surface | what is wrong | evidence | sev | status |
|---|---------|--------------|----------|-----|--------|
| D425 | agent ▸ context shelf | **A "place" chip is frozen in the language it was captured in.** With the app in Japanese, every other thing on the surface translated and the shelf still read `Grammar` / `Translate` / `Dictionary` / `Control Center` — in English, beside chips that had all become 場所 and 通常. It never corrects itself: `route` items are `retained`, so the capture-time language survives a restart. | Live. `document.documentElement.lang` **en → ja**, all **8** desk window titles Japanese (スクレイパー / ライブラリ / エージェント …), and a whole-window walk of the Agent surface's **45 controls** found **0** left in Latin script. Then the shelf, read item by item: `辞書 · 〜あとで`, **`場所 · Grammar`**, **`場所 · Translate`**, **`場所 · Dictionary`**, **`場所 · Control Center`**, `辞書 · 食べる`. Cause: `routeAgentContext` (`agentContextHandoff.ts:286`) resolves the section name through `t()` and the **result** is persisted; every one of its 10 call sites passes `t(AGENT_NAVIGATION_SECTION_LABEL_KEYS[section])`, so the string is correct exactly once. | P3 | **fixed `2c84464d`.** `agentContextDisplayLabel` resolves a bare `route` label from `source.app` on every render; the shelf renders through it. Three cases keep the stored string and each has its own test: any other kind (the user's own content — translating a looked-up word is the i18n policy's exact prohibition), a `settingsRouteAgentContext` item (its label names a settings **card**, so there is no section key), and an unresolvable key (printing `palette.section.grammar` would be worse than stale English). **Proven live in both directions:** still in Japanese the shelf became `文法 / 翻訳 / 辞書` while 〜あとで and 食べる were untouched; switched back to English it read `Grammar / Translate / Dictionary` again. 5 cases; mutations redden exactly the settings arm and exactly the user-content control. **A mutation that first read GREEN is recorded in the commit** — the settings case was passing through the *unresolved-key* fallback because the stub catalogue had no `palette.section.settings`, so the guard under test was never reached. **Residual, disclosed:** `Control Center` still does not translate. That is the settings-route arm behaving as designed — its id is `route:settings/visualizer`, so it names a settings page rather than a section. Fixing that half needs settings pages to carry a resolvable key on the item, which is a different change; it is the remaining work on this defect and is deliberately not guessed at here. |

**State restored, and checked rather than assumed:** the UI language is back to English
(`localStorage['ui-lang'] === 'en'`, all 8 window titles English again), the Settings window opened
for the walk was closed, and the desk is back to the 8 windows it booted with.
`/logs?level=error` **0 entries** throughout.

**One trap worth the two calls it cost.** `/eval` mangles a **non-ASCII literal in the JS body** —
`[...btns].find(b => b.textContent.trim() === '日本語')` matched nothing while the same button's
`textContent` read back as exactly `"日本語"` (length 3). Select such a control **by index**, never
by its own label. This is the `/type` Japanese trap in a route nobody had hit it in yet.
